, we find a finite
good presentation
(\Psi(w;t),F(w))
for
\Phi
. By induction, we find

\Xi(w;u)\supseteq\Psi
and
q\in\mathrm{S}_{x}^{\Delta,\Xi}(M)
which is

\mathcal{L}(A)
-quantifiable over
\mathcal{L}
and consistent with
p
and
X
. Since

w\subset z
, as stated in the first paragraph of the proof,
{\left.q\right|_{w}}

extends to a complete
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\mathbfcal{G}(A))
-definable
\mathcal{L}_{0}(M_{0})
-type.

By
Corollary

3.2.18
and
Lemma

3.4.5
, we now find an
\mathcal{L}(A)
-definable
r\in\mathrm{S}_{x}^{\Delta,\Xi,F}(M)
which is consistent with
q
and
X
. By
Lemmas

3.3.1
and
3.4.3
,
r
is
\mathcal{L}(A)
-quantifiable over
\mathcal{L}
.
∎

Let
(\varphi_{i}(z;t_{i}))_{i\in\omega}
enumerate all
\mathcal{L}_{0}
-formulas. By
3.5.2
, we find
\Theta_{i}
containing
\varphi_{i}
and
q_{i}\in\mathrm{S}^{\Delta,\Theta_{\leqslant i}}_{z}(M)
, which is
\mathcal{L}(A)
-quantifiable over
\mathcal{L}
and consistent with
p\cup\bigcup_{j<i}q_{j}
and
X
. Then
\bigcup_{i}q_{i}\in\mathrm{S}^{0}_{z}(M)
is
\mathcal{L}(A)
-definable and consistent with
p
and
X
. By
Proposition

3.3.4
and
Lemma

3.3.7
,
q
extends to a complete
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\mathbfcal{G}(A))
-definable
\mathcal{L}_{0}(M_{0})
-type.
∎

This result is already non-trivial when
X=\mathbf{K}^{x}
and
T=\mathrm{ACVF}
:

Corollary 3.5.3
.

Let
\Psi(x;t)
be a set of
\mathcal{L}_{0}
-formulas and
A=\mathrm{acl}_{0}(A)\leqslant M_{0}
. Any
\mathcal{L}_{0}(A)
-quantifiable
p\in\mathrm{S}_{x}^{\Psi}(M_{0})
can be extended to an
\mathcal{L}_{0}(A)
-definable
q\in\mathrm{S}^{0}_{x}(M_{0})
.∎

If we do not assume
(
\mathbf{E}^{\infty}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}})
, and try to replace the use of
Corollary

3.2.18
by that of
Lemma

3.2.21
, the above induction fails. We can, nevertheless, recover a local version of the result:

Proposition 3.5.4
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap\mathbf{K}^{\times}(M)
and
X\subseteq\mathbf{K}^{x}
be
\mathcal{L}(A)
-definable, where
A=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)\subseteq M^{\mathrm{eq}}
. Assume
(
\mathbf{C}_{V}
)
in both
M
and the pair
(M_{0},M)
,
(
\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
)
,
(
\mathbf{E}^{\infty}_{\mathbf{k}})
,
(
\mathbf{I}_{\mathbf{k}}
)
,
(
\mathbf{FR}_{n}
)
. Also assume that:

(
\mathbf{P}^{n}_{\pi(X)}
)

For every projection
Y\subseteq\mathbf{K}^{zy}
of
X
, with
|y|=1
,
N\succcurlyeq M
and
a\in N^{y}
,
Y_{a}
is
n
-prepared by some finite
\mathcal{L}_{0}(Ma)
-definable set
C\subseteq\mathbf{K}
.

Then, for every finite set
\Psi(x,t)
of
\mathcal{L}_{0}
-formulas, there exists an
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\mathbfcal{G}(A))
-definable
p\in\mathrm{S}_{x}^{\Psi}(M)
consistent with
X
.

Proof.

Let
A_{P}=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{P}}}(A)
. We say that
\Theta(zy,s)
, where
|y|=1
, is a hereditarily good presentation if
\Theta
is of the form
\Phi(z,t)\cup\{y\in F_{\lambda}(z)\}
for some good presentation
(\Phi,F)
where
\Phi
is itself an hereditarily good presentation.

Claim 3.5.5
.

Let
\Theta(x,s)
be a hereditarily good presentation and
q\in\mathrm{S}_{x}^{\Theta}(M_{0})
be
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\mathbfcal{G}(A_{P}))
-definable. Then for
\mathcal{L}\in\{\mathcal{L}_{0},\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{P}}\}
,
q
is
\mathcal{L}(\mathbfcal{G}(A_{P}))
-quantifiable over
\mathcal{L}
— in particular it has a complete
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\mathbfcal{G}(A_{P}))
-definable extension to
\mathrm{S}^{0}_{x}(M_{0})
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
|x|
. Since
(
\mathbf{E}^{\infty}_{\mathbf{k}})
holds both in
M_{0}
and, by
Lemma

2.3.19
, in the pair
(M_{0},M)
, quantifiability follows from
Lemmas

3.3.1
and
3.4.3
— applied respectively to
M_{0}
and to the pair
(M_{0},M)
. The existence of a complete definable extension follows by
Corollary

3.5.3
applied in
M_{0}
.
∎

We now prove, by induction on
x=zy
, the existence of
p\in\mathrm{S}_{x}^{\Psi}(M_{0})
which is
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\mathbfcal{G}(A_{P}))
-definable and consistent with
X
. By compactness, there exists
(G_{\omega})_{\omega\in\Omega}:\mathbf{K}^{z}\to\mathbf{K}^{[<\infty]}
such that the family
(X_{z})_{z}
is
n
-prepared by
G
. Let
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
bound the degree of any polynomial appearing in
\Psi
and
G
. By
Proposition

3.2.8
, and induction, we find a finite hereditarily good presentation
(\Theta(z,s),F(z))
for
\varphi_{d}(x,uv):=\mathrm{v}(\sum_{|I|<d}u_{I}x^{I})\geqslant\mathrm{v}(\sum_{|I|<d}v_{I}x^{I})
. By induction, there exists
q\in\mathrm{S}_{z}^{\Theta}(M_{0})
which is
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\mathbfcal{G}(A_{P}))
-definable and consistent with
X
. By
3.5.5
,
q
is
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{P}}(\mathbfcal{G}(A_{P}))
-quantifiable over
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{P}}
. By
Lemma

3.2.21
, there exists an
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{P}}(A_{P})
-definable
p\in\mathrm{S}^{\Theta,F}_{x}(M_{0})
consistent with
X
. By hypothesis,
(
\mathbf{C}_{V}
)
holds in
(M_{0},M)
, and so does
(
\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
)
, by
Corollary

2.3.17
. By
Proposition

3.3.4
, it follows that
{\left.p\right|_{\varphi_{d}}}
is
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\mathbfcal{G}(A_{P}))
-definable — and hence so is
{\left.p\right|_{\Psi}}
.

Let
a\in A_{P}
be the canonical basis of
{\left.p\right|_{\varphi_{d}}}
. Since
M_{0}=M^{\mathrm{a}}\subseteq\mathrm{acl}_{0}(M)
, we have
a\in\mathrm{acl}_{0}(M)
. Let
c\in\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(M)
be a code of the finite
\mathcal{L}_{0}(M)
-orbit of
a
— which is included in its finite
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{P}}(A)
-orbit. Let
f
be
\mathcal{L}_{0}
-definable such that
c\in f(M)
and
e\in M^{\mathrm{eq}}
be a code of
f^{-1}(c)
. The
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{P}}(A)
-orbit of
c
consists of finite subsets of the
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{P}}(A)
-orbit of
a
and is therefore finite. Hence, so is the
\mathcal{L}(A)
-orbit of
e
;
i.e.
,
e\in\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)=A
. It follows that
{\left.p\right|_{\varphi_{d},M}}\subseteq\bigcap_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}(M_{0}/M)}\sigma({\left.p\right|_{\varphi_{d}}})
is
\mathcal{L}(A)
-definable. By
Proposition

3.3.4
, it is in fact
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\mathbfcal{G}(A))
-definable — and hence so is
{\left.p\right|_{\Psi,M}}
.
∎

This local result does imply the existence of a global invariant type:

Corollary 3.5.6
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap\mathbf{K}^{\times}(M)
and

X\subseteq\mathbf{K}^{x}
be strict pro-
\mathcal{L}(A)
-definable, where
A=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)\subseteq M^{\mathrm{eq}}
. Assume
(
\mathbf{C}_{V}
)
in both
M
and the pair

(M_{0},M)
,
(
\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
)
,
(
\mathbf{E}^{\infty}_{\mathbf{k}})
,
(
\mathbf{I}_{\mathbf{k}}
)
,
(
\mathbf{FR}_{n}
)
and:

(
\mathbf{P}^{n}_{\pi(X)}
)

For every projection
Y\subseteq\mathbf{K}^{zy}
of
X

onto finitely many coordinates, with
|y|=1
, every
N\succcurlyeq M
and
every
a\in\mathbf{K}^{z}(N)
,
Y_{a}
is
n^{\ell}
-prepared by some

\mathcal{L}_{0}(Ma)
-definable set
C\subseteq\mathbf{K}(N)
, for some
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}
.

Then there exists an
\mathrm{Aut}(M/\mathbfcal{G}(A))
-invariant
p\in\mathrm{S}^{0}_{x}(M)
consistent with
X
.

Note that
(
\mathbf{FR}_{n}
)
implies
(
\mathbf{FR}_{n^{\ell}}
)
for every
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}
. Also if
M
is a finitely ramified henselian field,
(
\mathbf{C}_{V}
)
holds in both
M
and
(M_{0},M)
, since both theories have maximally complete models, and
(
\mathbf{P}^{n}_{\pi(X)}
)
holds by
Remark

3.2.20
.

Proof.

For every (finite) set
\Psi(x,t)
of
\mathcal{L}_{0}
-formulas, the set of
p\in\mathrm{S}^{0}_{x}(M)
which are consistent with
X
and whose
\Psi
-type is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/\mathbfcal{G}(A))
-invariant is closed. It is non-empty, by
Proposition

3.5.4
. By compactness, the intersection of all these sets, which coincides with the set of
\mathrm{Aut}(M/\mathbfcal{G}(A))
-invariant
p\in\mathrm{S}^{0}_{x}(M)
consistent with
X
, is also non-empty.
∎

Remark 3.5.7
.

If
T
is a
\mathbf{k}
-
\mathbf{\Gamma}
-enrichment of finitely ramified henselian fields,
then, by
Corollary

2.3.12
, the pair
(M_{0},M)
is elementarily equivalent
to one where both
\mathbf{K}
and
\mathbf{P}\mathbf{K}
are maximally complete — namely the pair
of the maximal completion of
M
inside the maximal completion of its
algebraic closure. Hence
(
\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{B}}
)
— and therefore
(
\mathbf{C}_{V}
)
— holds both in
M

and in the pair
(M_{0},M)
.

4.
Invariant completions

Notation 4.1
.

In this section, let
T
be an
\mathbf{RV}
-enrichment of the theory of characteristic zero henselian fields.

4.1.
Main results

Our goal in this section is to describe the behaviour of global
\mathcal{L}
-types whose underlying
\mathcal{L}_{0}
-type is invariant. A crucial point is that
2.2.7
can be reformulated in the following manner: for every
A\leqslant M\models T
,

\mathrm{tp}_{0}(A)\cup\mathrm{tp}(\mathbf{RV}(A))\vdash\mathrm{tp}(A).

Therefore, the main point of this section is to better understand
\mathrm{tp}(\mathbf{RV}(A))
and then deduce properties of
\mathrm{tp}(A)
. In particular, we will show that
\mathbf{RV}(A)
is generated by a small canonical set. This will allow us to conclude that a global type whose underlying quantifier free type is invariant is itself invariant over
\mathbf{RV}
(
cf.

Corollary

4.3.17
). However, a better control of the parameters requires more auxiliary sorts. Recall that

\mathbf{Lin}_{A}:=\bigsqcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\in\mathbf{S}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(A))\\
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\end{subarray}}s/\ell\mathfrak{m}s.

In this section, we prove:

Theorem 4.1.1
.

Assume

(
\mathbf{I}_{\mathbf{k}}
)

The residue field
\mathbf{k}
is infinite.

Let
M\preccurlyeq N\models T
sufficiently saturated and homogeneous,
A\subseteq\mathbfcal{G}(M)
and
a\in\mathbf{K}(N)
such that
\mathrm{tp}_{0}(a/M)
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant. Then the type
\mathrm{tp}(a/M)
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A\mathbf{RV}(M)\mathbf{Lin}_{A}(M))
-invariant.

4.2.
Invariance and stably embedded sets

Note that we consider invariance over large subsets of our model — that happen to be the points of some stably embedded definable sets. This gives rise to some subtle issues and two notions of invariance. When
D=\bigcup_{i}D_{i}
is ind-
\mathcal{L}
-definable, we denote by
D^{\mathrm{eq}}
the ind-
\mathcal{L}
-definable union of all
\mathcal{L}
-interpretable sets
X
that admit an
\mathcal{L}
-definable surjection
\prod_{j}D_{i_{j}}\to X
.

Definition 4.2.1
.

Let
M
be an
\mathcal{L}
-structure,
C\subseteq M
,
D
be a (ind-)
\mathcal{L}
-definable set and
p
be a partial
\mathcal{L}(M)
-type.
We say that
p
:

•

is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
-invariant
if for every
\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
,
p
and
\sigma(p)
are equivalent.

•

has
\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
-invariant
D
-germs
if it is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
-invariant and so is the
p
-germ of every
\mathcal{L}(M)
-definable map
f:p\to D^{\mathrm{eq}}
;

•

is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/D)
-invariant
if it has
\mathrm{Aut}(M/D(M))
-invariant
D
-germs.

We will only apply these notions for
M
saturated,
p
a complete
\Delta
-type for some set of
\mathcal{L}
-formulas
\Delta
and
C
equal to the
M
-points of a stably embedded (ind-)
\mathcal{L}
-definable set,
D
stably embedded — an ind-
\mathcal{L}
-definable set
D=\bigcup_{i}D_{i}
is stably embedded if any definable
X\subseteq\prod_{j}D_{i_{j}}
is definable with parameters from
D
.

Remark 4.2.2
.

(1)

A type might be
\mathrm{Aut}(M/D(M))
-invariant but not
\mathrm{Aut}(M/D)
-invariant. For example, let
M\models\mathrm{ACVF}
and
b
be a closed ball of
M
without any
\mathrm{acl}(\ulcorner b\urcorner\mathbf{RV}_{1}(M))
-definable subballs. Then any two
a_{1},a_{2}\in b(M)
have the same type over
\mathrm{acl}(\ulcorner b\urcorner\mathbf{RV}_{1}(M))
. However, for every
x\in b
,
\mathrm{rv}_{1}(x-a_{1})=\mathrm{rv}_{1}(x-a_{2})
implies that the
a_{i}
are in the same maximal open subball of
b
. It follows that the generic of
b
over
M
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/\ulcorner b\urcorner)
-invariant but not
\mathrm{Aut}(M/\ulcorner b\urcorner\mathbf{RV}_{1})
-invariant.

(2)

A type
p\in\mathrm{S}(M)
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
-invariant if and only if for every realization
a\models p
in a sufficiently homogeneous
N\succcurlyeq M
, any
\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
extends to an element of
\mathrm{Aut}(N/Ca)
.

(3)

On the other hand, a type
p\in\mathrm{S}(M)
has
\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
invariant
D
-germs, where
D
is stably embedded, if and only if for every
a\models p
in a sufficiently saturated
N\succcurlyeq M
, any
\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
extends to an element of
\mathrm{Aut}(N/CD(N)a)
—
cf.
the proof of
Lemma

4.2.4
.

(4)

The space of types with
\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
-invariant
D
-germs is closed: for any
\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
and
\mathcal{L}(M)
-definable map
f:p\to D^{\mathrm{eq}}
, no types in the open set
“
[f]_{p}\neq[f^{\sigma}]_{p}
”
has
\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
-invariant
D
-germs.

Let us now recall the following folklore result on stable embeddedness which states that we can recover the usual characterisation of types and hence of definable closure (equivalently internality) from invariance over a stably embedded definable set:

Lemma 4.2.3
.

Let
M
be saturated sufficiently large,
D
be (ind-)
\mathcal{L}
-definable stably embedded and
e\in M
. If
e
is fixed by every
\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}(M/D(M))
, then
e\in\mathrm{dcl}(D(M))
.

Proof.

Let
e^{\prime}\in M
be such that
e\equiv_{D(M)}e^{\prime}
. By
[
TZ12
, Lemma 10.1.5]
— more precisely its extension
mutatis mutandis
to stably embedded ind-definable sets — we can find
\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}(M/D(M))
such that
e^{\prime}=\sigma(e)=e
. Since
D
is stably embedded, there exists a small
A\subseteq D(M)
such that,
\mathrm{tp}(e/A)\vdash\mathrm{tp}(e/D(M))
. So both types have a single realisation in
M
,
i.e.
,
e\in\mathrm{dcl}(A)\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}(D(M))
.
∎

One advantage of the stronger notion of invariance is transitivity:

Lemma 4.2.4
.

Let
M\preccurlyeq N
be
\mathcal{L}
-structures with
N
saturated and sufficiently large,
C\subseteq M
(potentially large),
D
be an (ind-)
\mathcal{L}
-definable stably embedded set,
p\in\mathrm{S}(M)
have
\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
-invariant
D
-germs,
a\models p
in
N
and
q\in\mathrm{S}(N)
be
\mathrm{Aut}(N/CD(N)a)
-invariant. Then
{\left.q\right|_{M}}
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
-invariant.

If, moreover,
q
has
\mathrm{Aut}(N/CD(N)a)
-invariant
E
-germs, for some (ind-)
\mathcal{L}
-definable set
E
, then
{\left.q\right|_{M}}
has
\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
-invariant
E
-germs.

Proof.

Fix
\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
. Since
p
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
-invariant, the
automorphism
\sigma
extends to a partial
\mathcal{L}
-elementary isomorphism

\tau:M(a)\to M(a)
fixing
a
. Since
\sigma
fixes the germs of every
definable map from
p
to
D^{\mathrm{eq}}
,
\tau
induces the identity on

D^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathrm{dcl}(M(a)))
. It follows, since
D
is stably embedded that
Ca

and
\tau(Ca)
have the same type over
D(N)
and hence that
\tau

extends to an element of
\mathrm{Aut}(N/CD(N)a)
—
cf.

[
TZ12
, Lemma 10.1.5]
. This automorphism
\tau
fixes
q
. It follows
that
{\left.q\right|_{M}}
is fixed by
{\left.\tau\right|_{M}}=\sigma
.

If, moreover,
q
has
\mathrm{Aut}(N/CD(N)a)
-invariant
E
-germs, then
\sigma={\left.\tau\right|_{M}}
fixes the
{\left.q\right|_{M}}
-germ of any
\mathcal{L}(M)
-definable function into
E^{\mathrm{eq}}
.
∎

The core of our proof of
Theorem

4.1.1
is the following variation on transitivity:

Lemma 4.2.5
.

Let
M\preccurlyeq N\models T
,
C\subseteq M
potentially large,
a\in\mathbf{K}^{x}(N)
a (potentially infinite) tuple and
\rho:\mathbf{K}^{x}\to\mathbf{RV}
be pro-
\mathcal{L}_{0}(M)
-definable. Assume that
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(C\rho(a))
and that
p:=\mathrm{tp}_{0}(a/M)
and
[\rho]_{p}
are
\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
-invariant. Then
\mathrm{tp}(a/M)
has
\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
-invariant
\mathbf{RV}
-germs.

Proof.

Pick
\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}(M/C)
. Let
N_{0}\models\mathrm{ACVF}
containing
N
be
saturated and sufficiently large. By invariance of
p
, the automorphism

\sigma
extends to a partial
\mathcal{L}_{0}
-isomorphism
\tau:M(a)\to M(a)

fixing
a
. Note that
\tau(\rho(a))=\rho^{\sigma}(a)=\rho(a)
and hence

{\left.\tau\right|_{(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a)))}}
is the identity. By quantifier elimination
in
\mathrm{ACVF}
(in
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{RV}}
, which implies a strong form of stable embeddedness
for
\mathbf{RV}
),
\tau
extends to a partial elementary map which is the identity on

\mathbf{RV}(N_{0})
, which further extends to some element of

\mathrm{Aut}(N_{0}/C\mathbf{RV}(N_{0})a)
, also denoted
\tau
—
cf.

[
TZ12
, Lemma 10.1.5]
. By
2.2.7
,
\mathrm{tp}(Ma)=\mathrm{tp}(\sigma(M)a)
,

i.e.
,
\sigma(p)=p
. Moreover, any
\mathcal{L}(Ma)
-definable
X\subseteq\mathbf{RV}^{n}
is
\mathcal{L}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a)))
-definable and hence
X(N)=\tau(X(N))=X^{\tau}(N)
, equivalently,
\sigma
fixes the
p
-germ of any

\mathcal{L}(M)
-definable function into
\mathbf{RV}^{\mathrm{eq}}
.
∎

4.3.
Computing leading terms

In view of
Lemma

4.2.5
, given any
A
-invariant type
\mathrm{tp}_{0}(a/M)
, we want to find a pro-
\mathcal{L}_{0}(M)
-definable map
\rho
such that
\rho(a)

\mathrm{dcl}_{0}
-generates
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a))
and
[\rho]_{p}
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariants. When
A\preccurlyeq M
and
A
is sufficiently large, this is done in
Corollary

4.3.16
. As previously stated, for general small
A
, dealing with closed balls forces us to also consider maps into certain
A
-definable
\mathbf{k}
-vector spaces. The goal then becomes to build a
“nice”
model of
T
containing
A
and proceed by transitivity.

The technical core of the proof consists in a generalisation to relative arity one of the classical description of
1
-types in henselian fields, cf.
Lemmas

4.3.10
,
4.3.13
and
4.3.8
.

Let us start with three leading term computations that we will need later.

Lemma 4.3.1
.

Let
M\models\mathrm{ACVF}
. Let
L\leqslant K=\mathbf{K}(M)
be a subfield and
R\subseteq\mathrm{rv}_{1}(K)
contain
\mathrm{rv}_{1}(L)
. Let also
b\in\mathbf{B}(K)
,
g\in\mathbf{K}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(LR))
,
c\in K
and
P:=\prod_{i<d}(x-e_{i})\in\mathbf{K}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(LR))[x]
.

(1)

If
c,g\in b
and
e_{i}\nin b
, for all
i
, then
\mathrm{rv}_{1}(P(c))=\mathrm{rv}_{1}(P(g))\in\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(R)
.

(2)

If
c\nin b
and
g,e_{i}\in b
, for all
i
. then
\mathrm{rv}_{1}(P(c))=\mathrm{rv}_{1}(c-g)^{d}
.

(3)

Assume that
b
is closed, that
c,g,e_{i}\in b
, for all
i
, and that the maximal open
subball of
b
around
c
does not contain
g
nor any
e_{i}
. Then

\mathrm{rv}_{1}(P(c))=\bigoplus_{i\leqslant d}\mathrm{rv}_{1}(P_{i}(g))\mathrm{rv}_{1}(c-g)^{i}\in\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(R\mathrm{rv}_{1}(c-g)),

where
P(y+x)=\sum_{i}P_{i}(y)x^{i}
. In particular, the sum is well-defined.

In fact, computation (2) is a particular case of computation (3) — consider
the smallest closed ball containing
c
,
g
and the
e_{i}
. Recall that if

a_{1}
and
a_{2}
are in some ball
b
that does not contain
c
,

\mathrm{rv}_{1}(c-a_{1})=\mathrm{rv}_{1}(c-a_{2})
—
cf
.
Remark

2.2.4
.

Proof.

Let us first prove (
1
). We have
\mathrm{rv}_{1}(P(c))=\prod_{i}\mathrm{rv}_{1}(c-e_{i})=\prod_{i}\mathrm{rv}_{1}(g-e_{i})=\mathrm{rv}_{1}(P(g))\in\mathbf{RV}_{1}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(LR))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{1}(L)R)=\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(R)
, where the inclusion follows from quantifier elimination for
\mathrm{ACVF}
in
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{RV}}
. As for (
2
), we have
\mathrm{rv}_{1}(P(c))=\prod_{i}\mathrm{rv}_{1}(c-e_{i})=\mathrm{rv}_{1}(c-g)^{d}
.
Finally, in the case of (
3
), let
Q(x)=P((c-g)x+g)/(c-g)^{d}
and
\sum_{i}Q_{i}(y)x^{i}=Q(x+y)
. The roots
(e_{i}-g)/(c-g)
of
Q
are in
\mathcal{O}
. Thus
Q\in\mathcal{O}[x]
,
Q_{i}(0)=P_{i}(g)(c-g)^{i-d}\in\mathcal{O}
and
\mathrm{v}(Q(1))=0
. We have:

\displaystyle\mathrm{rv}_{1}(P(c))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{rv}_{1}(c-g)^{d}\mathrm{res}(Q(1))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{rv}_{1}(c-g)^{d}(\sum_{i}\mathrm{res}(Q_{i}(0)))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{rv}_{1}(c-g)^{d}(\bigoplus_{i}\mathrm{rv}_{1}(Q_{i}(0)))

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{i\leqslant d}\mathrm{rv}_{1}(P^{(i)}(g))\mathrm{rv}_{1}(c-g)^{i}

\displaystyle\in\mathbf{RV}_{1}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(LR\mathrm{rv}_{1}(c-g)))

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(R\mathrm{rv}_{1}(c-g)),

where the third equality follows from the fact that
\mathrm{v}(\sum_{i}Q_{i}(0))=\mathrm{v}(Q(1))=0\leqslant\min_{i}\{\mathrm{v}(Q_{i}(0))\}\leqslant\mathrm{v}(\sum_{i}Q_{i}(0))
.
∎

Remark 4.3.2
.

In mixed characteristic, we will be applying this result to the least equicharacteristic zero coarsening, yielding a computation for
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}
and not just
\mathrm{rv}_{1}
.

Essentially every computation of leading terms reduces to the above cases by the following lemma.

Lemma 4.3.3
.

Let
M\models\mathrm{ACVF}
,
L\leqslant K=\mathbf{K}(M)
,
c\in K
and
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(L)\leqslant R=\mathbf{RV}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(R))\leqslant\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(K)
. The following are equivalent:

(1)

\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(L(c))\subseteq R
;

(2)

For every
P\in\mathbf{K}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(LR))[x]
which is monic and irreducible over

\mathbf{K}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(LR))
, we have
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(P(c))\in R
.

Moreover if
P\in\mathbf{K}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(LR))[x]
is irreducible, then its roots are either
all inside or outside any
B\in\mathbf{B}^{[<\infty]}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(LR))
.

Proof.

By (1),
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(P(c))\in\mathbf{RV}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(LRc))\subseteq\mathbf{RV}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(L(c))R))=R
. The converse is a consequence of the fact that
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}
is a multiplicative morphism and any polynomial over
L
is a product of (an element of
L
and) monic irreducible polynomials over
\mathbf{K}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(LR))
.

As for the moreover statement, fix some
B\in\mathbf{B}^{[<\infty]}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(LR))
. Let
E
be the set of roots of
P
that belong to
B
and
Q=\prod_{e\in E}(x-e)\in\mathbf{K}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(LR))[x]
. If
P
is irreducible, then
Q=P
or
Q=1
.
∎

One last important ingredient — also ubiquitous in the development of motivic
integration (
e.g.
, in
[
HK06
]
) — is the fact that, in
characteristic zero, finite sets of points (and of balls in equicharacteristic
zero) can be canonically parametrised by
\mathbf{RV}
. Recall the definition of

\mathbf{B}^{[r]}
from
Definition

3.2.1
and that of
b[n]
and
B[n]
from

Definition

3.2.19
.

Lemma 4.3.4
.

For every
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, there exists
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that for every characteristic zero valued field
L
and
B\in\mathbf{B}^{[r]}(L)
with
|B[m]|=r
, there exists an
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\ulcorner B\urcorner)
-definable injection
\nu:B\to\mathbf{RV}^{n}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
r
. If
r=1
, take
m=1
and
\nu
to be constant equal to
1\in\mathbf{RV}_{1}
. If
|B|>1
, we may assume that
|B[m]|=r
for all
m
, the
Lemma
will follow by compactness. Also, assuming that
L\models\mathrm{ACVF}
is sufficiently saturated and homogeneous, it suffices to find an
\mathrm{Aut}(L/\ulcorner B\urcorner)
-invariant injection
\nu:B\to(\mathbf{RV}_{\infty})^{n}
. Indeed, since
B
is finite some projection to
\mathbf{RV}^{n}
is already injective and it must be definable. Finally, let
\gamma:=\max\{\mathrm{v}(b_{1}-b_{2}):b_{i}\in B
distinct
\}
. Since
|B[m]|=r
for all
m
,
\gamma<\mathrm{rad}(B)+\mathrm{v}(\mathbb{Z})
and
B
can be injected in the set of open
\mathrm{v}_{\infty}
-balls of radius
\gamma/\Delta_{\infty}
. So we may assume that the residue characteristic of
L
is zero.

Let
B^{\prime}
be the set of closed balls of radius
\gamma
around the balls of
B
. By construction, we have
|B^{\prime}|<|B|=r
. For every
b^{\prime}\in B^{\prime}
, let
B_{b^{\prime}}:=\{b\in B:b\subseteq b^{\prime}\}
. Note that, by hypothesis,
\mathrm{res}_{b^{\prime}}(b)\in\mathbf{R}_{b^{\prime}}=\{
maximal open subballs of
b^{\prime}\}
uniquely determines
b
inside
B
. Let
c_{b^{\prime}}\in\mathbf{R}_{b^{\prime}}
denote the average of the
\mathrm{res}_{b^{\prime}}(b)
as
b
ranges over
B_{b^{\prime}}
. By induction, we find an
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\ulcorner B\urcorner)
-definable injection
\mu:\{c_{b^{\prime}}:b^{\prime}\in B^{\prime}\}\to\mathbf{RV}^{n}
. For every
b\in B
, let
\nu(b):=(\mathrm{rv}_{1}(b-c_{b^{\prime}}),\mu(c_{b^{\prime}}))
where
b\subseteq b^{\prime}\in B^{\prime}
. Then
\nu:B\to\mathbf{RV}^{n+1}
is an
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\ulcorner B\urcorner)
-definable injection.
∎

Lemma 4.3.5
.

For every
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
there exists an
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that for every characteristic zero valued field
L
and every
B\in\mathbf{B}^{[r]}(L)
with
|B[m]|=r
, there exists
g\in\mathbf{K}^{[r]}(L)
with exactly one point inside each ball of
B[m]
.

Proof.

Let us start with a weaker version of the result:

Claim 4.3.6
.

For every
b\in\mathbf{B}(\mathrm{acl}_{0}(L))
,
b(\mathrm{acl}_{0}(L))\neq\emptyset
.

Proof.

We may assume that
L
is algebraically closed. If
v(L)\neq 0
,
L\models\mathrm{ACVF}
and hence, by model completeness,
b(L)\neq\emptyset
. If
\mathrm{v}(L)=0
,
\mathrm{rad}(b)\in\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(L))=\{0\}
. If
0\in b
, we are done. Otherwise,
\mathrm{v}(b)=0
and
b\subseteq\mathcal{O}
. So
b
is open and it is (interdefinable with) a residue element. But
\mathbf{k}(\mathrm{acl}_{0}(L))=\mathrm{res}(L)
and thus
b(L)\neq\emptyset
.
∎

Claim 4.3.7
.

For every
B\in\mathbf{B}^{[r]}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(L))
, there exists
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
g\in\mathbf{K}^{[r]}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(L))
such that, if
|B[m]|=r
, there is exactly one point of
g
inside each ball of
B[m]
.

Proof.

We may assume that
B
is irreducible over
L
—
i.e.
, for any non-empty
\mathcal{L}_{0}(L)
-definable
C\subseteq B
,
C=B
. For every
b\in B
, let
d\in b(\mathrm{acl}_{0}(L))
. Let
D
be
\mathcal{L}_{0}(L)
-definable and irreducible over
L
containing
d
and let
g_{b}\in\mathrm{acl}_{0}(L)
be the average of
D\cap b
. Then
g_{b}\in b[m]
, where
m:=|D\cap b|
. Let
g
be finite
\mathcal{L}_{0}(L)
-definable set irreducible over
L
containing
g_{b}
. Since
|B[m]|=r=|B|
, we get
g\cap b[m]=\{g_{b}\}
. By irreducibility, each ball of
B[m]
contains exactly one element of
g
.
∎

The lemma follows by compactness.
∎

Let
\mathbf{B}^{[r]}_{x}
denote the (ind-
\mathcal{L}_{0}
-definable) set of
\mathcal{L}_{0}
-definable maps
F:\mathbf{K}^{x}\to\mathbf{B}^{[r]}
and
\mathbf{B}^{[<\infty]}_{x}
denote the (ind-
\mathcal{L}_{0}
-definable) set
\bigcup_{r}\mathbf{B}^{[r]}_{x}
. Similarly we denote
\mathbf{K}^{[r]}_{x}
the (ind-
\mathcal{L}_{0}
-definable) set of
\mathcal{L}_{0}
-definable maps
F:\mathbf{K}^{x}\to\mathbf{K}^{[r]}
and
\mathbf{K}^{[<\infty]}_{x}
the (ind-
\mathcal{L}_{0}
-definable) set
\bigcup_{r}\mathbf{K}^{[r]}_{x}
.

Notation 4.2
.

We fix
M\preccurlyeq N\models T
,
A\subseteq M^{\mathrm{eq}}
,
a\in\mathbf{K}^{x}(N)
a
potentially infinite tuple and
c\in\mathbf{K}(N)
a single element. Assume that

p(xy)=\mathrm{tp}_{0}(ac/M)
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant and let
q:=\mathrm{tp}_{0}(a/M)
.
For every
F,G\in\mathbf{B}^{[<\infty]}_{x}(M)
, we write
F\leqslant_{q}G
if

q(x)\vdash F^{\cup}(x)\subseteq G^{\cup}(x)
. Finally, let
E:=\{F\in\mathbf{B}^{[<\infty]}_{x}(M):p(x,y)\vdash y\in F^{\cup}(x)\}
.

In the following
Lemmas

4.3.8
,
4.3.10
and
4.3.13
, we will describe how
\mathbf{RV}(M(ac))
is generated depending on the shape of
E
.

Lemma 4.3.8

(Finite sets)
.

Assume that
E
has a least element
f
for
\leqslant_{q}
and that

f\in\mathbf{K}^{[r]}_{x}
. Then, there exists a pro-
\mathcal{L}_{0}(M)
-definable map

\rho:\mathbf{K}^{xy}\to\mathbf{RV}^{n}
, whose
p
-germ is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant such
that
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(ac))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a))\rho(ac))
.

Proof.

Let
\rho(ac)=\nu_{a}(c)
, where
\nu_{a}:f(a)\to\mathbf{RV}^{n}
is the

\mathcal{L}_{0}(f(a))
-definable injection of
Lemma

4.3.4
. By invariance of

p
, for every
\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
,
c\in f^{\sigma}(a)\cap f(a)
. Since

f
is the least element of
E
, we have
f^{\sigma}(a)=f(a)
and hence

[f]_{q}
— and thus
[\rho]_{p}
— is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant.
Moreover, since
c\in\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(Ma\rho(ac))
, we have
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(ac))\subseteq\mathbf{RV}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(Ma\rho(ac)))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(Ma)\rho(ac))
.
∎

We now assume that
E\cap\mathbf{K}^{[<\infty]}_{x}=\emptyset
.

Lemma 4.3.9
.

There exists a pro-
\mathcal{L}_{0}(M)
-definable map

\nu:\mathbf{K}^{xy}\to\mathbf{RV}^{\xi}
, with
\xi
potentially infinite, whose

p
-germ is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant such that, for every
F\in E
, for some

m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
which only depends on
|F(a)|
, the ball
b\in F(a)[m]
containing
c
is
\mathcal{L}_{0}(Ma\nu(ac))
-definable, and

\nu(ac)\in\mathrm{acl}_{0}(Ma)
.

Proof.

For every
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, let
m_{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
be as in
Lemma

4.3.4
. Let
F\in E\cap\mathbf{B}^{[r]}_{x}
be irreducible over
q
and such that
|F[m_{r}]|=r
— if such an
F
does not exist let
\nu_{r}(x)=1
. By irreducibility, for every
G\in E\cap\mathbf{B}^{[r]}_{x}
with
G\leqslant_{q}F
, every ball in
F(a)
contains exactly one ball of
G(a)
. In particular, neither
\gamma=\max\{\mathrm{v}(b_{1}-b_{2}):b_{i}\in F(a)
distinct
\}
nor
B_{r}(a)
, the set of open balls of radius
\gamma+\mathrm{v}(m_{r})
around balls of
F(a)
, depend on the choice of
F
. It follows that
[B_{r}]_{q}
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant. By construction, inclusion induces an injection
F(a)\to B_{r}(a)
and that
|B_{r}(a)|=|B_{r}(a)[m_{r}]|
.

Ss in
Lemma

4.3.4
, let
\nu_{r}:B_{r}(a)\to\mathbf{RV}^{n}
be an
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\ulcorner B_{r}(a)\urcorner)
-definable injection. Let
\nu_{r}(ac)=\nu_{r}(b)
where
c\in b\in B_{r}(a)
. Note that
\nu_{r}(ac)\in\mathrm{acl}_{0}(Ma)
. The element of
F(a)
containing
c
is uniquely determined by
b
, and hence by
\nu_{r}(ac)
; and
[\nu_{r}]_{p}
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant by construction.

Let us now fix any
F\in E
that we can assume irreducible. Let
M=\max\{m_{s}:s\leqslant|F(a)|\}
. The sequence
|F(a)[M^{k}]|\geqslant 1
is decreasing, bounded by
|F(a)|
and hence, there exists
k\leqslant|F(a)|
such that
|F(a)[M^{k}][M]|=|F(a)[M^{k+1}]|=|F(a)[M^{k}]|
. Let
r:=|F[M^{k}]|
. By the previous paragraphs and the choice of
M
, the ball
b\in F(a)[M^{k}]
containing
c
is
\mathcal{L}_{0}(Ma\nu_{r}(ac))
-definable. It follows that
\nu=(\nu_{r})_{r\geqslant 1}
has the required properties.
∎

We now wish to consider the case where, either
E
induces a strict intersection in the least equicharacteristic zero coarsening
\mathrm{v}_{\infty}
(case (1)), or
c
is generic over
Ma
in a finite set of open
\mathrm{v}_{\infty}
-balls (case (2)):

Lemma 4.3.10

(Open and strict balls)
.

Assume that one of the following holds:

(1)

For all
F\in E
, there exists
G\in E
with
G[m]<_{q}F
, for any
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
;

(2)

There exists an
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
such that for every
F\in E
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, there exists an open
G\in E\cap\mathbf{B}^{[r]}_{x}
with
G[m]\leqslant_{q}F
.

Then, there is a pro-
\mathcal{L}_{0}(M)
-definable map
\rho:\mathbf{K}^{xy}\to\mathbf{RV}^{\xi}
whose
p
-germ is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant and such that
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(ac))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a))\rho(ac))
.

Note that the cases (1) and (2) are not mutually exclusive.

Proof.

Let
\nu:\mathbf{K}^{xy}\to\mathbf{RV}^{\xi}
be as in
Lemma

4.3.9
.

Claim 4.3.11
.

For every
F\in E
, the ball
b\in F(a)
containing
c
is in
\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(Ma\nu(ac))
.

Proof.

Assume that there exist
G\in E
with
G[m]\leqslant_{q}F
, for every
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. Then, by
Lemma

4.3.9
applied to
G
, the ball
b^{\prime}\in G(a)[m]
containing
c
is
\mathcal{L}_{0}(Ma\nu(ac))
-definable. The claim follows since
b^{\prime}\subseteq b
.

Otherwise, by case (2), we can find a minimal
r
such that for every
F\in E
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, there exists an open
G\in E\cap\mathbf{B}^{[r]}_{x}
with
G[m]\leqslant_{q}F
. Then for
m
sufficiently large, depending on
r
, by
Lemma

4.3.9
, the ball
b^{\prime}\in G[m]
containing
c
is
\mathcal{L}_{0}(Ma\nu(ac))
-definable. The claim follows since
b^{\prime}\subseteq b
.
∎

If there does not exist
g\in\mathbf{K}^{[<\infty]}_{x}(M)
such that
\emptyset<_{q}g\leqslant_{q}E
, let
\rho(ac)=\nu(ac)
. If such a
g
exists, we may assume that it is irreducible and, then, the cardinality of the
F\in E
irreducible over
q
is bounded by
|g(a)|
. Let
F\in E
be irreducible over
q
of maximal cardinality
r
and let
b(ac)\in F(a)
contain
c
. Note that the partition of
g(a)
induced by
F
, and in particular
h(ac):=g(a)\cap b(ac)
, does not depend on
F
. Moreover, since there is some (irreducible)
G\in E\cap\mathbf{B}^{[r]}_{x}
with
G[|h(ac)|]\leqslant_{q}F
, the average of
h(ac)
is in
b(ac)
. So, replacing
h(ac)
by its average, we may assume that
h(ac)
is a singleton. Then,
h(ac)\in\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(g(a)b(ac))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(Ma\nu(ac))
by
4.3.11
. Let
\rho(ac)=(\nu(ac),\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(c-h(ac)))
.

For every
\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
, applying the previous argument to
g
and
F^{\sigma^{-1}}\in E
, we have
h(ac)\in b^{\sigma^{-1}}(ac)
, and hence, by invariance of
p
,
h^{\sigma}(ac)\in b(ac)
. Note that the smallest (closed) ball containing
h(ac)
and
h^{\sigma}(ac)
is algebraic over
Ma
and let
D(a)
be its finite orbit over
Ma
. We have
D\leqslant_{q}E
. If
D[m]\in E
, for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, then there is
G\in E
open (irreducible) such that
G[m]\leqslant_{q}D[m]
and hence
D>_{q}G\in E
. But then either
h(ac)\nin G(ac)
or
h^{\sigma}(ac)\nin G
. By invariance of
p
, we may assume that
h(ac)\nin G(ac)
, contradicting the fact that
h(ac)
does not depend on the choice of
F
. Thus
D(a)[m]\not\in E
and so
c\nin D(a)[m]^{\cup}
. It follows that
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(c-h(ac))=\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(c-h^{\sigma}(ac))
. We have just proved the
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariance of
[\rho]_{p}
.

Now, to prove that
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(ac))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a))\rho(ac))
, by
Lemma

4.3.3
, it suffices to prove that, for every irreducible
P\in\mathbf{K}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(Ma\nu(ac)))[x]
,
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(P(c))\in\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a))\rho(ac))
. Recall that, by
Lemma

4.3.9
,
\nu(ac)\in\mathrm{acl}_{0}(Ma)
. Let
z(a)
be the finite set, irreducible over
M(a)
, containing the set
Z
of roots of
P
. If
z\leqslant_{q}E
, then, as above
c
avoids
d(ac)[m]
, where
d(ac)
is the smallest closed ball around
Z\cup\{h(ac)\}
. By
Lemma

4.3.1
.(
2
), taking into account
Remark

4.3.2
,
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(P(c))=\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(c-h(ac))^{d}\in\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\rho(ac))
. Otherwise, there is some
F\in E
such that
Z\cap F(a)^{\cup}=\emptyset
. Then, by
Lemma

4.3.1
.(
1
),
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(P(c))\in\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a))\nu(ac))
.
∎

Remark 4.3.12
.

There are actually two distinct possible behaviours in
Lemma

4.3.10
:

•

If there does does not exist
g\in\mathbf{K}^{[<\infty]}_{x}
such that
g\leqslant_{q}F
for every
F\in E
, then
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(ac))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a))\nu(ac))\subseteq\mathrm{acl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a)))
;

•

If such a
g
exists, then
\mathrm{v}(M(ac))\not\subseteq\mathrm{acl}_{0}(\mathrm{v}(M(a)))
.

The last remaining case to consider is when
c
is generic over
Ma
in some closed
\mathrm{v}_{\infty}
-ball. For every
B\in\mathbf{B}^{[<\infty]}
, we define
\mathbf{R}_{B,m}:=\{b^{\prime}\subseteq B^{\cup}:b^{\prime}
open ball of radius
\mathrm{rad}(B)+\mathrm{v}(m)\}
and
\mathbf{R}_{B,\infty}=\varprojlim_{m}\mathbf{R}_{B,m}
. For every
x\in B^{\cup}
, let
\mathrm{res}_{B,m}(x)
denote the unique element of
\mathbf{R}_{B,m}
containing
x
and
\mathrm{res}_{B,\infty}:B^{\cup}\to\mathbf{R}_{B,\infty}
be the induced map.

Lemma 4.3.13

(Closed balls)
.

Assume that there exists an
F\in E
such that for every
g\in\mathbf{K}^{[<\infty]}_{x}(M)
with
g\leqslant_{q}F
,
c\nin\mathrm{res}_{F(a),\infty}(g(a))^{\cup}
. Let
b\in F(a)
contain
c
,
\xi\in\mathbf{RV}^{n}
such that
b\in\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(Ma\xi)
and
G\in\mathrm{res}_{b,\infty}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(Ma\xi))
. Then
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(ac))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a))\xi\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(\mathrm{res}_{F(a),\infty}(c)-G(a\xi)))
.

In later applications of this lemma, we will take
\xi=\nu(ac)
as given by
Lemma

4.3.9
.

Proof.

Note that for any
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, the hypothesis on
F
remains true of
F[m]
. So, replacing
F
by some
F[m]
, with
|F[m](a)|
minimal, we may assume that
|F[m](a)|
is constant. By
Lemma

4.3.5
, we can now find
f\in\mathbf{K}^{[<\infty]}_{x}(M)
such that
f(a)
has exactly one point in every ball of
F(a)
. By hypothesis,
c\nin\mathrm{res}_{F(a),\infty}(f(a))
. Let
h\in\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(Ma\xi)
denote the unique element of
f(a)\cap b
. Since
\mathrm{rad}(F(a))/\Delta_{\infty}=\mathrm{v}_{\infty}(c-h)=\mathrm{v}_{\infty}(c-G(a,\xi))\leqslant\mathrm{v}_{\infty}(G(a,\xi)-h)
, we have
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(c-h)=\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(\mathrm{res}_{F(a),\infty}(c)-G(a\xi))\oplus\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(G(a\xi)-h)
. So it suffices to prove that
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(ac))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a))\xi\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(c-h))
.

By
Lemma

4.3.3
, it further suffices to prove that, for every irreducible
P\in\mathbf{K}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(Ma\xi))[x]
,
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(P(c))\in\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a))\xi\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(c-h))
. If, for every
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, no root of
P
is in
b[m]
, then, by
Lemma

4.3.1
.(
1
),
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(P(c))\in\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a))\xi)
. Otherwise, let
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
be such that every root of
P
is in
b[m]
. Since
\mathbf{K}(\mathrm{acl}_{0}(Ma\xi))\subseteq\mathrm{acl}_{0}(Ma)
, let
z(a)
be finite irreducible over
M(a)
containing the roots of
P
. By hypothesis,
c\nin\mathrm{res}_{F(a)[m]}(z(a))
. By
Lemma

4.3.1
.(
3
),
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(P(c))\in\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a))\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(c-h))
.
∎

Notation 4.3
.

Let
\widehat{A}\subseteq\mathbf{K}(M)
contain a realisation of every
\mathcal{L}(A)
-type and assume that
M
is sufficiently saturated and homogeneous.

We can now wrap up the relative arity one case:

Proposition 4.3.14
.

There exists a pro-
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\widehat{A})
-definable map
\rho:\mathbf{K}^{xy}\to\mathbf{RV}^{\xi}
such that
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(ac))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a))\rho(ac))
.

Proof.

Note first that any
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant
p
-germ of
\mathcal{L}_{0}(M)
-definable functions is represented by an
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\widehat{A})
-definable function — it suffices to consider a realisation in
\widehat{A}
of the type of the parameters over
A
.

If
E\cap\mathbf{K}^{[<\infty]}_{x}\neq\emptyset
, we apply
Lemma

4.3.8
. So let us assume that
E\cap\mathbf{K}^{[<\infty]}_{x}=\emptyset
. If for every
F\in E
, there exists
G\in E
with
G[m]<_{q}F
, for every
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we are in case (1) of
Lemma

4.3.10
and we can conclude. So we may assume that there exists
F
such that for every
G\in E
,
F\leqslant_{q}G[m]
, for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. If there exists
g\in\mathbf{K}^{[<\infty]}_{x}
with
g\leqslant_{q}F
and
c\in\mathrm{res}_{F(a),\infty}(g(a))
, then, for all
H\in E
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
,
H\leqslant_{q}F[m]
, for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. Let
G(a):=\mathrm{res}_{F(a),mn}(g(a))
, then
c\in G[n]\leqslant_{q}H
and hypothesis (2) of
Lemma

4.3.10
holds with
r=|g|
.

So we may assume that no such
g
exist,
i.e.
, the hypotheses of
Lemma

4.3.13
hold. As previously, we may assume that
|F[m]|
is constant. Let
\nu
be as in
Lemma

4.3.9
; we may assume that
\nu
is
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\widehat{A})
-definable. Let
b(ac)\in F(a)
contain
c
.

Claim 4.3.15
.

There exists
G(ac)\in\mathrm{res}_{b(a)[m],\infty}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\widehat{A}a\nu(ac)))
, for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.

Proof.

By construction of
\widehat{A}
, there exists
\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
such that
F^{\sigma}
is
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\widehat{A})
-definable. By
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariance of
p
, we have
F^{\sigma}\in E
and hence
F^{\sigma}\leqslant_{q}F[m]
, for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. So, up to replacing
F
by
F^{\sigma}
, we may assume
F
is
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\widehat{A})
-definable. By
Lemma

4.3.5
, and replacing
F
by some
F[m]
, we find
g\in\mathbf{K}^{[<\infty]}_{x}(\widehat{A})
with exactly one element in each ball of
F
. It then suffices to consider the only element of
\mathbf{k}_{F(a),\infty}(g(a))
contained in
b(ac)
.
∎

By
Lemma

4.3.13
,
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(ac))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a))\nu(ac)\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(\mathrm{res}_{F(a),\infty}(c)-G(ac)))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(Ma)\widehat{A}ac)
.
∎

Corollary 4.3.16
.

There exists a pro-
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\widehat{A})
-definable map
\rho:\mathbf{K}^{x}\to\mathbf{RV}^{\xi}
, such that
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(a))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M)\rho(a))
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on an enumeration of
a
. The induction step is
Proposition

4.3.14
and the limit case is trivial.
∎

Corollary 4.3.17
.

The type
\mathrm{tp}(a/M)
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/\widehat{A}\mathbf{RV})
-invariant.

Proof.

It follows from
Corollary

4.3.16
and
Lemma

4.2.5
.
∎

4.4.
Invariant resolutions

Notation 4.4
.

Let
M\preccurlyeq N\models T
both be sufficiently saturated and homogeneous and
A\subseteq\mathbfcal{G}(M)
.

By transitivity, there remains to build a sufficiently saturated model containing
A
whose type is invariant.

Lemma 4.4.1
.

Assume that
A\subseteq\mathbf{K}(M)
and let
R\subseteq\mathbf{RV}(M)
. There exists
C\subseteq\mathbf{K}(N)
and a pro-
\mathcal{L}_{0}(M)
-definable map
\rho:\mathbf{K}^{|C|}\to\mathbf{RV}^{\xi}
such that
R\subseteq\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(A(C))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(AR)
,
q:=\mathrm{tp}_{0}(C/M)
and
[\rho]_{q}
are
\mathrm{Aut}(M/AR)
-invariant and
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(C))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M)\rho(C))
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on an enumeration of
R
. Assume the property holds of
R
for some
C
and
\rho
and pick any
\zeta\in\mathbf{RV}_{\infty}(M)
. If
\zeta\in\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(\mathrm{acl}_{0}(AC))
, let
c\in\mathbf{K}(\mathrm{acl}_{0}(AC))
be such that
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(c)=\zeta
. Let
D
be a minimal finite
\mathcal{L}_{0}(AC\zeta)
-definable set containing
c
. Replacing
c
by the average of
D
, we may assume that
c\in\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(AC\zeta)\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(MC)\subseteq N
. We have
R\zeta\subseteq\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(A(Cc))\subseteq\mathbf{RV}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(AC\zeta))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(A(C))\zeta)\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(AR\zeta)
,
\mathrm{tp}_{0}(Cc/M)
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/AR\zeta)
-invariant and, since
c\in\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(MC)=M(C)^{\mathrm{h}}
,
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(Cc))=\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(C))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M)\rho(C))
.

If
\zeta\nin\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(\mathrm{acl}_{0}(AC))
, let
c\in N
be generic in
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}^{-1}(\zeta)
over
M
. Then
p:=\mathrm{tp}_{0}(Cc/M)
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/AR\zeta)
-invariant. By
Lemma

4.3.10
, we find a pro-
\mathcal{L}_{0}(M)
-definable map
\rho^{\prime}:\mathbf{K}^{|C|+1}\to\mathbf{RV}_{\infty}^{\xi}
such that
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(Cc))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(C))\rho^{\prime}(Cc))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M)\rho(C)\rho^{\prime}(Cc))
and whose
p
-germ is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/AR\zeta)
-invariant. Moreover, no root of any
P\in\mathbf{K}(\mathrm{dcl}(AC))[x]
is in
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}^{-1}(\zeta)
. For any
g\in\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}^{-1}(\zeta)
, by
Lemma

4.3.1
.(
1
),
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(P(c))=\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(P(g))
does not depend on
g
and is thus in
\mathbf{RV}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(AC\zeta))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(AC)\zeta)\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(AR\zeta)
. By
Lemma

4.3.3
,
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(A(Cc))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(AR\zeta)
.
∎

Corollary 4.4.2
.

Assume that
A\subseteq\mathbf{K}(M)
. There exists
C\preccurlyeq N
containing
A

and a pro-
\mathcal{L}_{0}(M)
-definable map
\rho:\mathbf{K}^{|C|}\to\mathbf{RV}^{\xi}
,
such that
p:=\mathrm{tp}_{0}(C/M)
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A\mathbf{RV}(M))
-invariant,

[\rho]_{p}
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A\mathbf{RV}(M))
-invariant and

\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(MC)\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M)\rho(C))
.

In particular, by
Lemma

4.2.5
,
\mathrm{tp}(C/M)
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A\mathbf{RV})
-invariant.

Proof.

Let
A\subseteq M_{1}\preccurlyeq M
, with
M_{1}
small. Applying
Lemma

4.4.1
to

\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M_{1})
, we find
C\subseteq N
and
\rho
such that

\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M_{1})\subseteq\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(A(C))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(A\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M_{1}))\cap\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(N)\subseteq\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M_{1})
,
p:=\mathrm{tp}_{0}(C/M)
and
[\rho]_{p}
are
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M_{1}))
-invariant
and
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(C))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M)\rho(C))
. By replacing

C
with
\mathbf{K}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(AC))
, we may assume
A\subseteq C=C^{\mathrm{h}}
. Since

\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(C)=\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(A(C))=\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M_{1})\preccurlyeq\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(N)
and

C
is a characteristic zero henselian field, it follows from
2.2.7

that
C\preccurlyeq N
.
∎

Corollary 4.4.3
.

Assume that
A\subseteq\mathbf{K}(M)
. There exists
\widehat{A}\preccurlyeq N

containing a realisation of every
\mathcal{L}(A)
-type such that

\mathrm{tp}(\widehat{A}/M)
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A\mathbf{RV})
-invariant.

Proof.

Let
C
be as in
Corollary

4.4.2
. Then any
\mathcal{L}(A)
-definable set
X
has
a point in
C
and and its type is an
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A\mathbf{RV})
-invariant type
concentrating on
X
. Since the set of
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A\mathbf{RV})
-invariant types is
closed, it follows by compactness that any
\mathcal{L}(A)
-type has an

\mathrm{Aut}(M/A\mathbf{RV})
-invariant extension. The corollary follows by the standard
construction relying on transitivity,
Lemma

4.2.4
— for the limit steps,
note that
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A\mathbf{RV})
-invariance is finitary:
\mathrm{tp}(c/M)
is

\mathrm{Aut}(M/A\mathbf{RV})
-invariant if and only if for every finite
c_{0}\subseteq c
,

\mathrm{tp}(c_{0}/M)
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A\mathbf{RV})
-invariant.
∎

Recall that, by
2.1
,
\mathbf{Lin}_{A}(M)
denotes the set of cosets
c+\ell\mathfrak{m}s
where
s\in\mathbf{S}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(A))
has a basis in
M
and
c\in s(M)
.

Lemma 4.4.4
.

Assuming:

(
\mathbf{I}_{\mathbf{k}}
)

The residue field
\mathbf{k}
is infinite in models of
T
,

there exists
C\subseteq\mathbf{K}(N)
and an
\mathcal{L}_{0}(A)
-definable map
\rho:\mathbf{K}^{|C|}\to\mathbf{Lin}_{A}^{\xi}
such that, for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
,
\mathbf{S}_{n}(A)\subseteq s_{n}(C)
,
\mathrm{tp}_{0}(C/M)
is
\mathcal{L}_{0}(A)
-definable and
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(C))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M)\mathbf{Lin}_{A}(M)\rho(C))
.

In mixed characteristic, we may further assume that
\bigcup_{n>0}\mathbf{T}_{n}(A)\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(C)
.

Proof.

Fix
s\in\mathbf{S}_{n}(A)
and let
\beta\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(M)
be a basis of
s
. Then, any
\alpha\models\beta\cdot({\left.\eta_{\mathcal{O}}\right|_{M}})^{\otimes n^{2}}
— which is realised in
N
by
(
\mathbf{I}_{\mathbf{k}}
)
— where
\eta_{\mathcal{O}}
is the generic (quantifier free) type of
\mathcal{O}
, is a basis of
s
. Note that
\mathrm{tp}_{0}(\alpha/M)=\beta\cdot\eta_{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes n^{2}}
only depends on
s
and is indeed
\mathcal{L}_{0}(A)
-definable. Let
\overline{\alpha}
, respectively
\overline{\beta}
, be the basis of
s/\mathfrak{m}_{\infty}s
— seen as a pro-definable set— induced by
\alpha
, respectively
\beta
. The matrix of
\mathbf{k}_{\infty}
-coefficients of
\alpha
in the basis
\beta
is
\mathrm{res}_{\infty}(\beta^{-1}\cdot\alpha)
where
\beta^{-1}\cdot\alpha\models({\left.\eta_{\mathcal{O}}\right|_{M}})^{\otimes n^{2}}
. It follows that
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(\alpha))=\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(\beta^{-1}\cdot\alpha))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathbf{RV}(M)\mathrm{res}_{\infty}(\beta^{-1}\cdot\alpha))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathbf{RV}(M)\overline{\beta}\overline{\alpha})
. The first part of the statement follows by iterating the above construction independently for every
s\in\mathbf{S}_{n}(A)
.

Let us now assume that we are in mixed characteristic. For every
c\models{\left.\eta_{\mathfrak{m}}\right|_{M}}
,
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(Cc))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(C))\mathrm{res}_{\infty}(c))
. This also holds for all maximal open subballs of
\mathcal{O}
. So, enlarging
C
further, we may also assume that
\mathbf{k}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(AC))\cap M\subseteq\mathrm{res}(C)
. Then, for every
e\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(A)
,
s=\tau_{n}(e)\in\mathbf{S}(A)
has a basis in
C
and every coordinate of
e
in that basis is the residue of an element of
C
. It then follows that
e\in t_{n}(C)
.
∎

Lemma 4.4.5
.

In equicharacteristic zero, assuming that, for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
,
\tau_{n}(\mathbf{T}_{n}(A))\subseteq s_{n}(\mathbf{K}(A))
. Then there exists
C\subseteq\mathbf{K}(N)
and an
\mathcal{L}_{0}(M)
-definable map
\rho:\mathbf{K}^{|C|}\to\mathbf{RV}^{\xi}
such that
A\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(C)
,
q:=\mathrm{tp}_{0}(C/M)
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant,
[\rho]_{q}
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant and
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(C))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M)\rho(C))
.

Proof.

For every
e\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(A)
, by hypothesis,
s:=\tau(e)
has a basis in
\mathbf{K}(A)
. It follows that
s/\mathfrak{m}s
also has a basis of
\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(A)
-points and hence is
\mathcal{L}_{0}(A)
-definably isomorphic to
\mathbf{k}^{n}
. By
Lemma

4.4.1
applied to
R:=\mathbf{k}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(A))\cap M\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(A)
, we find
\mathbf{K}(A)\subseteq C\leqslant\mathbf{K}(N)
such that
\mathbf{k}(\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(A))\cap M\subseteq\mathrm{res}(C)
,
q:=\mathrm{tp}_{0}(C/M)
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant,
[\rho]_{q}
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant and
\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M(C))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(\mathrm{rv}_{\infty}(M)\rho(C))
. Then, we have
A=\mathbf{K}(A)\cup\bigcup_{n}\mathbf{S}_{n}(A)\cup\bigcup_{n}\mathbf{T}_{n}(A)\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(C)
.
∎

Corollary 4.4.6
.

Assume that
(
\mathbf{I}_{\mathbf{k}}
)
holds. Then there exists
C\subseteq\mathbf{K}(N)
such that
\mathrm{tp}_{0}(C/M)
has
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A\mathbf{RV}(M)\mathbf{Lin}_{A}(M))
-invariant
\mathbf{RV}
-germs and
A\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(C)
.

Proof.

In mixed characteristic, this follows immediately from
Lemmas

4.2.5
and
4.4.4
. In residue characteristic zero, it follows from
Lemmas

4.4.4
and
4.4.5
and transitivity,
cf.

Lemma

4.2.4
.∎

Proof of
Theorem

4.1.1
.

By
Corollary

4.4.6
, we find
C\subseteq\mathbf{K}(N)
such that
A\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}_{0}(C)
and
\mathrm{tp}(C/M)
has
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A\mathbf{RV}(M)\mathbf{Lin}_{A}(M))
-invariant
\mathbf{RV}
-germs. Let
M\preccurlyeq M_{1}\preccurlyeq N
be sufficiently saturated and homogeneous and contain
C
. By
Corollary

4.4.3
, we find
\widehat{C}\preccurlyeq N
containing a realisation of every
\mathcal{L}(C)
-type such that
\mathrm{tp}(\widehat{C}/M_{1})
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M_{1}/C\mathbf{RV})
-invariant. Let
M_{1}\preccurlyeq M_{2}\preccurlyeq N
be sufficiently saturated and homogeneous and contain
\widehat{C}
.

Let
p:=\mathrm{tp}_{0}(a/M)
, which is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A)
-invariant by assumption.

Claim 4.4.7
.

\mathrm{tp}(a/M)\cup{\left.p\right|_{M_{2}}}
is consistent.

Proof.

Let
\varphi(x,m)
be some
\mathcal{L}(M)
-formula such that
N\models\varphi(a,m)
and let
\psi(x,d)\in{\left.p\right|_{M_{2}}}
. Let
\sigma\in\mathrm{Aut}(N/Amd)
be such that
\sigma(d)\in M
. Then
N\models\psi(a,\sigma(d))
and hence
\sigma^{-1}(a)\models\psi(x,d)\wedge\varphi(x,m)
.
∎

Let
a^{\prime}\models\mathrm{tp}(a/M)\cup{\left.p\right|_{M_{2}}}
. Then
\mathrm{tp}_{0}(a^{\prime}/M_{2})={\left.p\right|_{M_{2}}}
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M_{2}/C)
-invariant and thus, by
Corollary

4.3.17
,
\mathrm{tp}(a^{\prime}/M_{2})
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M_{2}/\widehat{C}\mathbf{RV})
-invariant. Since
\mathrm{tp}(\widehat{C}/M_{1})
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M_{1}/C\mathbf{RV})
-invariant, by transitivity,
cf.

Lemma

4.2.4
,
\mathrm{tp}(a^{\prime}/M_{1})
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M_{1}/C\mathbf{RV})
-invariant. By transitivity, since
\mathrm{tp}(C/M)
has
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A\mathbf{RV}(M)\mathbf{Lin}_{A}(M))
-invariant
\mathbf{RV}
-germs,
\mathrm{tp}(a/M)=\mathrm{tp}(a^{\prime}/M)
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/A\mathbf{RV}(M)\mathbf{Lin}_{A}(M))
-invariant.
∎

Let us conclude this section by relating
Theorem

4.1.1
to imaginaries:

Proposition 4.4.8
.

Let
T\supseteq\mathrm{Hen}_{0}
be an
\mathcal{L}
-theory, such that:

(
\mathbf{D}
)

For every strict pro-
\mathcal{L}(A)
-definable
X\subseteq\mathbf{K}^{x}
, with
A=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)\subseteq M^{\mathrm{eq}}\models T^{\mathrm{eq}}
, there exist an
\mathrm{Aut}(M/\mathbfcal{G}(A))
-invariant
p\in\mathrm{S}^{0}_{x}(M)
consistent with
X
;

(
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{K}}
)

For every tuple
a\in\mathbf{K}(M)
, with
M\models T
,
\mathrm{tp}_{1}(f(a))\vdash\mathrm{tp}(a)
, where
f:\mathbf{K}\to\mathbf{K}^{x}
is pro-
\mathcal{L}
-definable and
\mathcal{L}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{L}
such that
\mathcal{L}_{1}
is an
\mathbf{RV}
-enrichment of
\mathcal{L}_{0}
;

(
\mathbf{I}_{\mathbf{k}}
)

The residue field
\mathbf{k}
is infinite;

(
\mathbf{SE}
)

\mathbf{RV}
and
\mathbf{R}=\bigcup_{\ell}\mathcal{O}/\ell\mathfrak{m}
are stably embedded.

Let
M\models T
,
e\in M^{\mathrm{eq}}
and
A=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(e)
. Then

e\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf{K}(A)\cup(\mathbf{RV}\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)})^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)).

Proof.

Let
M\models T
be saturated and sufficiently large,
e\in M^{\mathrm{eq}}
and
A=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(e)
. Then
e=g(a)
for some
\mathcal{L}
-definable map
g
and tuple
a\in\mathbf{K}(M)
. Let
Y=g^{-1}(e)
and
X=f(Y)
, which is a strict pro-
\mathcal{L}(A)
-definable set. By
(
\mathbf{D}
)
, there exists an
\mathrm{Aut}(M/\mathbfcal{G}(A))
-invariant
p\in\mathrm{S}^{0}_{x}(M)
consistent with
X
. We may assume that
f(a)\models p
. By
Theorem

4.1.1
,
\mathrm{tp}_{1}(f(a)/M)
is
\mathrm{Aut}(M/\mathbfcal{G}(A)\mathbf{RV}(M)\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)}(M))
-invariant. By
(
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{K}}
)
,
\mathrm{tp}(a/M)
— and hence
e\in M^{\mathrm{eq}}
— is also
\mathrm{Aut}(M/\mathbfcal{G}(A)\mathbf{RV}(M)\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)}(M))
-invariant.

Since
\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)}
is a collection of free
\mathcal{O}/\ell\mathfrak{m}
-modules,
\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)}
and, in fact
\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)}\cup\mathbf{RV}
, is stably embedded. By
Lemma

4.2.3
,
e\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbfcal{G}(A)\cup\mathbf{RV}(M)\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)}(M))
—
i.e.
,
e=h(c)
for some
\mathcal{L}(\mathbfcal{G}(A))
-definable map
h
and tuple
c\in\mathbf{RV}^{m}(M)\times\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)}^{n}(M)
. Let
Z=h^{-1}(e)
. Then
\ulcorner Z\urcorner\in(\mathbf{RV}\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)})^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)
and

\displaystyle e\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbfcal{G}(A)\ulcorner Z\urcorner)

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbfcal{G}(A)\cup(\mathbf{RV}\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)})^{\mathrm{eq}}(A))

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf{K}(A)\cup(\mathbf{RV}\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)})^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)),

since
\mathbfcal{G}(A)\setminus\mathbf{K}(A)\subseteq\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)
.
∎

5.
Imaginaries in short exact sequences

In this section we will establish results which yield a relative understanding of imaginaries in certain pure short exact sequences of modules.

5.1.
The core case

We start with a well known lemma. We include a proof for convenience.

Lemma 5.1.1
.

Let
D
and
C
be stably embedded (ind-)definable sets in
D\cup C
, such that
D\perp C
(i.e.,
D
and
C
are orthogonal).

(1)

Assume that both
D
and
C
(considered with the full induced structures) weakly eliminate imaginaries. Then
D\cup C
weakly eliminates imaginaries.

(2)

Assume that both
D
and
C
eliminate imaginaries and that in
C
one has
\mathrm{dcl}=\mathrm{acl}
. Then
D\cup C
eliminates imaginaries.

Proof.

(1) Let
X\subseteq D^{m}\times C^{n}
be a definable subset. Since
D\perp C
, the

equivalence relation
\sim
on
C^{n}
, given by
c\sim c^{\prime}:\Leftrightarrow X_{c}=X_{c^{\prime}}
, has finitely many equivalence classes
Z_{1}=c_{1}/\sim,\ldots,Z_{k}=c_{k}/\sim
. As
\sim
is
\ulcorner X\urcorner
-definable and
C
is stably embedded, the
Z_{i}
are all
C^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\ulcorner X\urcorner))
- definable.

For
i=1,\ldots,k
, set
Y_{i}=X_{c_{i}}\subseteq D^{m}
, which is
D^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\ulcorner X\urcorner))
-definable since
D
is stably embedded. As
D
and
C
weakly eliminate imaginaries, there are finite tuples
d\in D(\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\ulcorner X\urcorner))
and
c\in C(\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\ulcorner X\urcorner))

such that the
Y_{i}
are all
d
-definable and the
Z_{i}
are all
c
-definable. Thus
X=\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}(Y_{i}\times Z_{i})
is
dc
-definable, so in particular
D(\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\ulcorner X\urcorner))C(\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\ulcorner X\urcorner)
-definable.

(2) The assumptions on
C
yield
C^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\ulcorner X\urcorner))\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(C(\ulcorner X\urcorner))
, and so the sets
Z_{1},\ldots Z_{k}
are
c
-definable for some
c\in C(\ulcorner X\urcorner)
. In particular
the
Y_{i}
are then all
\ulcorner X\urcorner
-definable, thus
D^{\mathrm{eq}}(\ulcorner X\urcorner)
-definable. By elimination of imaginaries in
D
, we find
d\in D(\ulcorner X\urcorner)
such that all

Y_{i}
are
d
-definable. We now finish as in (1).
∎

Fact 5.1.2
.

Let
G
be a group, and let
H_{1},\ldots,H_{N}
be subgroups of
G
. Then the left cosets of the
H_{i}
form a pre-basis of
closed sets for a noetherian topology on
G
. Moreover, setting
H_{I}:=\bigcap_{i\in I}H_{i}
for
I\subseteq\{1,\ldots,N\}
, the irreducible
closed sets for this topology are precisely the left cosets of those
H_{I}
with the property that any proper subgroup of
H_{I}
of the
form
H_{J}
is of infinite index in
H_{I}
.

Proof.

This is an easy consequence of Neumann’s Lemma.
∎

Let
R
be an integral domain,
\mathcal{L}\supseteq\mathcal{L}_{R-\mathrm{mod}}
and
M
an
\mathcal{L}
-expansion of an infinite torsion free
R
-module. Let
Z\subseteq M^{n}
be an
\mathcal{L}(M)
-definable set. We set
\dim_{R}(Z):=\max\{\dim_{R}(c/M):c\in Z(N)\}
, where
N\succcurlyeq M
is sufficiently saturated and
\dim_{R}(a/B)
denotes the
Q(R)
-linear dimension of
a
over
B
, for
Q(R)
the field of fractions of
R
.

Lemma 5.1.3
.

In the above situation, assume
\dim_{R}(Z)\leqslant r
. Then there are definable sets
C_{1},\ldots,C_{s}\subseteq M^{n}
with the following properties:

(1)

\bigcup_{i=1}^{s}C_{i}
is
\ulcorner Z\urcorner
-definable.

(2)

Each
C_{i}
is
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\ulcorner Z\urcorner)
-definable.

(3)

Each
C_{i}
is of the form
\gamma_{i}+H_{i}
, where
H_{i}
is
a definable
R
-submodule of
M^{n}
given by a condition
L_{i}x^{\prime}=mx^{\prime\prime}
, for some
matrix
L_{i}\in R^{(n-r)\times r}
and
m\in R\setminus\{0\}
, where
x
is the
tuple
x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}
up to permutation and
|x^{\prime}|=r
. (In particular,
\dim_{R}(C_{i})=r

for all
i
.)

(4)

Z\subseteq\bigcup_{i=1}^{s}C_{i}

Moreover, if
\mathcal{Z}
is a definable family such that

\dim_{R}(\mathcal{Z}_{b})\leqslant r
for all
b
, there are finitely many

R
-submodules
H_{1},\ldots,H_{N}
as in the statement such that for any
b
the

C_{i}
may be chosen among the cosets of the
H_{k}
.

Proof.

Since
\dim_{R}(Z)\leqslant r
, any
c\in Z
is in a set of the form
\gamma+H
as
in (
3
). By compactness there are sets
C_{1},\ldots,C_{N}
with

C_{i}=\delta_{i}+H_{i}
as in (
3
) such that

Z\subseteq\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}C_{i}
. Note that all
H_{i}
are
\emptyset
-definable
subgroups.

By Fact
5.1.2
, the cosets of the
H_{i}
form a pre-basis of closed sets for a noetherian topology on
M^{n}
. In particular, in this topology there exists a smallest closed subset

W
of
M^{n}
containing
Z
, and this
W
is clearly
\ulcorner Z\urcorner
-definable. The (finitely many) irreducible components of
W
are then all
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\ulcorner Z\urcorner)
-definable. If
W_{j}
is such an irreducible component, it is of the form

W_{j}=\gamma_{j}+\bigcap_{i\in I_{j}}H_{i}
(where
I_{j}\neq\emptyset
in case
r<n
, since
Z\subseteq\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{i}+H_{i}
by assumption). As the
H_{i}
are
\emptyset
-definable, it is easy to see that if we replace each component
W_{j}=\gamma_{j}+\bigcap_{i\in I_{j}}H_{i}
by
W_{j}^{\prime}=\bigcup_{i\in I_{j}}\gamma_{j}+H_{i}
, then the union of all
W_{j}^{\prime}
is
\ulcorner Z\urcorner
-definable
and each coset
\gamma_{j}+H_{i}
occurring in this union is
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\ulcorner Z\urcorner)
-definable.

The moreover part follows by compactness.
∎

Theorem 5.1.4
.

Let
R
be an integral domain and
M
be

0\rightarrow\mathbf{A}\rightarrow\mathbf{B}\rightarrow\mathbf{C}\rightarrow 0

a short exact sequence of
R
-modules, in an
\mathbf{A}
-
\mathbf{C}
-enrichment
\mathcal{L}
of the pure (in the sense of model theory) three sorted sequence of
R
-modules. Assume the following properties hold:

(1)

\mathbf{A}
is a pure submodule of
\mathbf{B}
(in the sense of module theory),

(2)

\mathbf{C}
is torsion free,

(3)

For any
l\in R\setminus\{0\}
, the quotient
\mathbf{C}/l\mathbf{C}
is finite and the preimage in
\mathbf{B}
of any coset
c+l\mathbf{C}
contains an element which is algebraic over
\emptyset
.

Let
e\in M^{\mathrm{eq}}
. Then, setting
E:=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(e)
and
\Delta:=\mathbf{C}(E)
, we have

e\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{eq}}(E)\mathbf{B}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{eq}}(E)),

where
\mathbf{B}_{\Delta}
denotes the union of all fibers

\mathbf{B}_{\delta}
with
\delta\in\Delta
.

We will prove a a slight generalization of Theorem
5.1.4
, namely the following Theorem
5.1.5
.

Theorem 5.1.5
.

Let
\tilde{R}
be a ring and
R=\tilde{R}/I
an integral domain, with
I
a finitely generated ideal.
Let
M
be

0\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{A}}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{C}}\rightarrow 0

a short exact sequence of
\tilde{R}
-modules, in an
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}
-
\tilde{\mathbf{C}}
-enrichment
\mathcal{L}
of the pure (in the sense of model theory) three sorted sequence of
\tilde{R}
-modules. Let
\mathbf{A}=\{a\in\tilde{\mathbf{A}}:Ia=(0)\}
, and let
\mathbf{B}
and
\mathbf{C}
be the
\tilde{R}
-submodules of
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{C}}
defined similarly. Consider
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B},\mathbf{C}
as
R
-modules in the natural way.

Assume the following properties hold:

(1)

\tilde{\mathbf{A}}
is a pure
\tilde{R}
-submodule of
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}
(in the sense of module theory),

(2)

\mathbf{C}=\tilde{\mathbf{C}}
,

(3)

\mathbf{C}
is a torsion free
R
-module,

(4)

For any
l\in R\setminus\{0\}
, the quotient
\mathbf{C}/l\mathbf{C}
is finite and the preimage in
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}
of any coset
c+l\mathbf{C}
contains an element which is algebraic over
\emptyset
.

Let
e\in M^{\mathrm{eq}}
. Then, setting
E:=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(e)
and
\Delta:=\mathbf{C}(E)
, we have

e\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{eq}}(E)\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{eq}}(E)),

where
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta}
denotes the union of all

\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\delta}
for
\delta\in\Delta
.

Proof.

Denote
\iota:\tilde{\mathbf{A}}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{B}}
and
v:\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{C}}
the structural maps.

Note that in particular
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}=\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{0}\subseteq\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta}
.
By (
1
),
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{C}}
are (purely) stably
embedded in
T
, with
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}\perp\tilde{\mathbf{C}}
. Indeed, since sufficiently
saturated
\tilde{R}
-modules are pure-injective
(
[
Pre88
, Corollary 2.9]
), replacing
M
by a sufficiently saturated
extension, the purity assumption (
1
) entails that the short exact
sequence of
\tilde{R}
-modules is split. Adding a splitting to the structure
yields an expansion in which
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}
is just the product structure of

\tilde{\mathbf{A}}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{C}}
. Thus, with a splitting,
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}
and

\tilde{\mathbf{C}}
are stably embedded and orthogonal. They remain so without the
splitting. For any subgroup
\Delta\leqslant\mathbf{C}
, as
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta}
is internally

\tilde{\mathbf{A}}
-internal, it follows from Lemma
2.5.18
that

\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta}
is stably embedded in
T
(over
\Delta
), and one has

\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta}\perp\tilde{\mathbf{C}}
.

Since
I
is finitely generated,
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}
and
\mathbf{C}
are definable and the
induced sequence of
R
-modules

0\rightarrow\mathbf{A}\rightarrow\mathbf{B}\rightarrow\mathbf{C}\rightarrow 0

is also exact.
Indeed, this is first order expressible and holds in any sufficiently saturated
elementary extension which is split.

We now fix some
e\in M^{\mathrm{eq}}
and let
X
be
\mathcal{L}(M)
-definable with

\ulcorner X\urcorner=\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(e)
. Lifting it to
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}
, we may assume
X\subseteq\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{n}
. Assume
\dim_{R}(v(X))=r
. Up to passing to some subset of
X

which is definable over
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\ulcorner X\urcorner)=E
, we may assume, using
Lemma
5.1.3
, that there are
L\in R^{(n-r)\times r}
,
m\in R\setminus\{0\}
and
\delta^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbf{C}^{n-r}
such that for
x=x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}
with

|x^{\prime}|=r
, we have
mv(x^{\prime\prime})=Lv(x^{\prime})+\delta^{\prime\prime}
for any
x=x^{\prime}x^{\prime\prime}\in X
. We now
consider the (fiberwise) action of
(\mathbf{A}^{r},+)
on
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{n}
given by

a\cdot(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}):=(x^{\prime}+ma,x^{\prime\prime}+La).

For every
c\in\mathbf{C}^{n}
, let
X_{c}
denote
the fiber above
c
,
i.e.

X_{c}=X\cap v^{-1}(c)
.

Claim 5.1.6
.

There is
l\in R\setminus\{0\}
such that

\dim_{R}(\{z\in v(X):(l\mathbf{A})^{r}\cdot X_{z}\neq X_{z}\})<r.

Proof.

Let
\pi(z)
be the partial type expressing that
z\in v(X)
and that

\dim_{R}(z/M)=r
, and let
\tau(y)
be the partial type expressing that
y\in(l\mathbf{A})^{r}
for any
l\in R\setminus\{0\}
. Fix a realization

(c,a)\models\pi(z)\cup\tau(y)
in
N\succcurlyeq M
, with
c=c^{\prime}c^{\prime\prime}
. Then there is
an
R
-linear map
\theta:\mathbf{C}(N)\rightarrow\mathbf{A}(N)
which is trivial on
\mathbf{C}(M)

and such that
\theta(c^{\prime})=ma
. Indeed, such a map
\theta
may be found as the
restriction of an
R
-linear map from
\mathbf{C}(N)\otimes_{R}Q(R)
to
\mathbf{A}(N)
which is
the identity on a supplement of
Q(R)c^{\prime}
and such that, for every non zero

l\in R
,
\theta(l^{-1}c^{\prime})=a_{l}
where the sequence
(a_{l})_{l\in R\smallsetminus\{0\}}
is a coherent sequence of roots: we have
a_{1}=ma
and every non zero
l,s\in R
,
sa_{sl}=a_{l}
.

For
b\in\tilde{\mathbf{B}}(N)
, let
\rho(b):=b+\theta(v(b))
. Then
\rho
is an
automorphism the
\tilde{R}
-module
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}(N)
whose inverse is
b\mapsto b-\theta(v(b))
, since
v(\rho(b))=\rho(b)
. It is the identity
on
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}(N)
and on
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}(M)
, since
\theta
is trivial on

v(\mathbf{C}(M))
. Moreover, it induces the identity on
\mathbf{C}=\tilde{\mathbf{B}}/\tilde{\mathbf{A}}
. Since
M
is an
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}
-
\tilde{\mathbf{C}}
-enrichment of the
pure short exact sequence,
\rho
preserves all the structure,

i.e.

\rho\in\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathcal{L}}(N/M)
.

In particular, for any
b\in X_{c}
, we have
\mathrm{tp}(b/M)=\mathrm{tp}(\rho(b)/M)
and so

\rho(b)\in X_{c}
. On the other hand, as
c^{\prime\prime}=(Lc^{\prime}+\delta^{\prime\prime})/m=Lc^{\prime}/m+\delta^{\prime\prime}/m

and
v(b)=(c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime})
, using
L\circ\theta=\theta\circ L
we compute

\theta(v(b))=(\theta(c^{\prime}),\theta(c^{\prime\prime}))=(\theta(c^{\prime}),\theta(Lc^{\prime}/m)+\theta(\delta^{\prime\prime}/m))=(ma,La),

from which it follows that

\rho(b)=b+\theta(v(b))=(b^{\prime}+\theta(c^{\prime}),b^{\prime\prime}+\theta(c^{\prime\prime}))=(b^{\prime}+ma,b^{\prime\prime}+La)=a\cdot(b^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime}).

Thus
X_{c}
is stabilized by
\bigcap_{l\in R\setminus\{0\}}(l\mathbf{A})^{r}
.

5.1.6
now follows by compactness.
∎

Fix
l\in R\setminus\{0\}
as in the claim. Let
X_{0}=\{x\in X:(l\mathbf{A})^{r}\cdot x\subseteq X\}
. Then
\dim_{R}(v(X\smallsetminus X_{0}))<r
and
X\smallsetminus X_{0}
is coded by induction. So we can assume that
X=X_{0}
is globally
stabilized by
(l\mathbf{A})^{r}
. In addition, using
assumption (
4
) and cutting
X
in finitely many pieces,
we may suppose that
v(X)\subseteq ml\mathbf{C}^{n}
. Indeed, there are only finitely
many cosets of
ml\mathbf{C}^{n}
, all
\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\emptyset)
-definable, so we may assume

X\subseteq v^{-1}(W)
for a coset
W
of
ml\mathbf{C}^{n}
. Replacing
X
by
X-h
for
some
h\in W\cap\mathrm{acl}(\emptyset)
if necessary, we may assume
W=ml\mathbf{C}^{n}
. Let

a\in\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{r}
and
c\in\mathbf{C}^{n}
. If there exist
b_{1}=b_{1}^{\prime}b_{1}^{\prime\prime}\in X_{c}
and

b_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{B}^{r}
such that
b_{1}^{\prime}=a+mlb_{0}^{\prime}
, we set

Y_{a,c}:=\{b^{\prime\prime}-lLb_{0}^{\prime}:(b_{1}^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime})\in X\}=X_{(b_{1}^{\prime})}-lLb_{0}^{\prime},

where
X_{(b_{1}^{\prime})}
denotes the fiber
\{b^{\prime\prime}\in\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{n-r}:(b_{1}^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime})\in X\}
. Else we set
Y_{a,c}:=\emptyset
. Let
us first show that in the first case,
Y_{a,c}
does not depend on the choice of

b_{1}
and
b_{0}^{\prime}
. Indeed, if
d_{1}=d_{1}^{\prime}d_{1}^{\prime\prime}\in X_{c}
and
d_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{B}^{r}
are such
that
d_{1}^{\prime}=a+mld_{0}^{\prime}
, then
b_{1}^{\prime}-d_{1}^{\prime}=ml(b_{0}^{\prime}-d_{0}^{\prime})
, so
mlv(b_{0}^{\prime}-d_{0}^{\prime})=0
,
thus
v(b_{0}^{\prime}-d_{0}^{\prime})=0
,
i.e.
,
b_{0}^{\prime}-d_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{A}^{r}
. Set

a_{0}^{\prime}:=l(b_{0}^{\prime}-d_{0}^{\prime})
. For
d^{\prime\prime}\in X_{(d_{1}^{\prime})}
, since
(l\mathbf{A})^{r}\cdot X_{c}=X_{c}
, we have

a_{0}^{\prime}\cdot(d_{1}^{\prime},d^{\prime\prime})=(d_{1}^{\prime}+ma_{0}^{\prime},d^{\prime\prime}+La_{0}^{\prime})=(b_{1}^{\prime},d^{\prime\prime}+La_{0}^{\prime})\in X_{c},

so
d^{\prime\prime}+lL(b_{0}^{\prime}-d_{0}^{\prime})=d^{\prime\prime}+La_{0}^{\prime}\in X_{(b_{1}^{\prime})}
, showing that
X_{(d_{1}^{\prime})}-lLd_{0}^{\prime}\subseteq X_{(b_{1}^{\prime})}-lLb_{0}^{\prime}
. By symmetry, we get the other inclusion
X_{(b_{1}^{\prime})}-lLb_{0}^{\prime}\subseteq X_{(d_{1}^{\prime})}-lLd_{0}^{\prime}
, thus
X_{(b_{1}^{\prime})}-lLb_{0}^{\prime}=X_{(d_{1}^{\prime})}-lLd_{0}^{\prime}
.

Let
\delta^{\prime\prime}=(\delta_{i}^{\prime\prime})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant n-r}
and let
b^{\prime}=a+mlb_{0}^{\prime}
be as in the definition of
Y_{a,c}
. Then for
y=b^{\prime\prime}-lLb_{0}^{\prime}\in Y_{a,c}
, we compute

mv(y)=mv(b^{\prime\prime})-mlLv(b_{0}^{\prime})=(Lv(b^{\prime})+\delta^{\prime\prime})-Lv(b^{\prime})=\delta^{\prime\prime},

yielding
Y_{a,c}\subseteq\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\delta^{\prime\prime}/m}=\prod_{i=1}^{n-r}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\delta_{i}^{\prime\prime}/m}
. It follows that

Y\subseteq(\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\delta^{\prime\prime}/m}\times\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{r})\times\mathbf{C}^{n}.

As

\delta^{\prime\prime}/m\in\mathbf{C}(E)=\Delta
and
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta}\perp\mathbf{C}
, by
Lemma
5.1.1
(1),
Y
is coded in
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta}^{\mathrm{eq}}\cup\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{eq}}
. So
X
is coded in the same sorts once the following claim is
established.

Claim 5.1.7
.

\ulcorner X\urcorner
and
\ulcorner Y\urcorner
are interdefinable.

It is clear by construction that
Y
is
\ulcorner X\urcorner
-definable. For the converse, we will use that we have reduced to the case where
v(X)\subseteq ml\mathbf{C}^{n}
. We may thus reconstruct
X
from
Y
as follows:

d=d^{\prime}d^{\prime\prime}\in X\Leftrightarrow\exists a\in\tilde{E}^{r}\,\exists d_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{B}^{r}:\,d^{\prime}=a+mld_{0}^{\prime}\text{ and }d^{\prime\prime}\in Y_{a,v(d)}+lLd_{0}^{\prime}

This yields the claim.
∎

5.2.
Some variants

We will now state two variants of Theorem
5.1.5
, tailor made for our applications to (enriched) henselian valued fields.

Variant 5.2.1
.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a multisorted language,
\mathcal{A}\sqcup\{\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\}\sqcup\mathcal{C}
a partition of the sorts of
\mathcal{L}
and let

\tilde{\mathbf{A}}\in\mathcal{A}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{C}}\in\mathcal{C}
. Let
\tilde{R}
be a ring and
R=\tilde{R}/I
an integral domain, with
I
a finitely generated ideal.
Let

(5.1)

0\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{A}}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{C}}\rightarrow 0

be a short exact sequence of
\tilde{R}
-modules. Let
M
be an
\mathcal{L}
-structure which is an
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{C}
-enrichment of the pure (in the sense of model theory) sequence of
\tilde{R}
-modules (
5.1
).
Assume that the properties (
1
)-(
4
) from the statement of Theorem
5.1.5
hold.

Let
e\in M^{\mathrm{eq}}
. Then, setting
E:=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(e)
and
\Delta:=\mathbf{C}(E)
, we have

e\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathcal{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathrm{eq}}(E)\cup(\mathcal{A}\cup\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta})^{\mathrm{eq}}(E)),

where
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta}
denotes the union of all

\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\delta}
for
\delta\in\Delta
.

Proof.

The proof is a slight variation of the proof of Theorem
5.1.5
. Let us indicate the necessary adaptations.

Firstly, it follows from the assumptions that
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{C}
are (purely) stably embedded with
\mathcal{A}\perp\mathcal{C}
. Thus, by Lemma
2.5.18
,
(\mathcal{A}\cup\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta})\perp\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{A}\cup\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta}
is stably embedded.

Given
e\in M^{\mathrm{eq}}
, we choose an
\mathcal{L}(M)
-definable set
X\subseteq\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\times\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{n}
with
e=\ulcorner X\urcorner
, where
\mathbf{A}^{\prime}
is a finite product of sorts from
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathbf{C}^{\prime}
is a finite product of sorts from
\mathcal{C}
. For
(a^{\prime},c^{\prime})\in\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{C}^{\prime}
, let

{\vphantom{X}}{}_{(a^{\prime},c^{\prime})}X\subseteq\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{n}

be the fiber over
(a^{\prime},c^{\prime})
. Performing the same reductions as in the proof of Theorem
5.1.5
, by compactness, we may assume that there is an
\emptyset
-definable set

Y\subseteq\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\times\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\delta}\times\mathbf{C}^{n}

for some finite tuple
\delta\in\Delta
such that for any
(a^{\prime},c^{\prime})\in\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{C}^{\prime}
,
\ulcorner{\vphantom{X}}_{(a^{\prime},c^{\prime})}X\urcorner
and
\ulcorner{\vphantom{Y}}_{(a^{\prime},c^{\prime})}Y\urcorner
are interdefinable, so in particular,
\ulcorner X\urcorner
and
\ulcorner Y\urcorner
are interdefinable. The result then follows
from Lemma
5.1.1
,
since
(\mathcal{A}\cup\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta})\perp\mathcal{C}
.
∎

The second variant is designed for applications to henselian valued fields in mixed characteristic.

Variant 5.2.2
.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a multisorted language,
\mathcal{A}\sqcup\{\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\}\sqcup\mathcal{C}
a partition of the sorts of
\mathcal{L}
. For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let

\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n}\in\mathcal{A}
, and let
\tilde{C}\in\mathcal{C}
. Let
\tilde{R}
be a ring and
R=\tilde{R}/I
an integral domain, with
I
a finitely generated ideal.
Let
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}=(\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}=(\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be projective systems of
\tilde{R}
-modules with surjective transition functions, let
\mathbf{C}=(\tilde{\mathbf{C}}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be the projective system with

\tilde{\mathbf{C}_{n}}=\tilde{\mathbf{C}}
for all
n
and identical transition functions. Let

(5.2)

0\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{A}}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{C}}\rightarrow 0

be a short exact sequence of projective systems of
\tilde{R}
-modules.

Let
M
be an
\mathcal{L}
-structure which is an
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{C}
-enrichment of the pure (in the sense of model theory) sequence of projective systems of
\tilde{R}
-modules (
5.2
).

Assume that for very
n\in\mathbb{N}
the exact sequence
0\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{n}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{n}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{C}}\rightarrow 0
satisfies the properties (
1
)-(
4
) from the statement of Theorem
5.1.5
.

Let
e\in M^{\mathrm{eq}}
. Then, setting
E:=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(e)
and
\Delta:=\mathbf{C}(E)
, we have

e\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\tilde{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{eq}}(E)(\mathcal{A}\cup\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta})^{\mathrm{eq}}(E)),

where
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta}
denotes the union of all

(\tilde{\mathbf{B}_{n}})_{\delta}
for
\delta\in\Delta
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Let us first show that
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{C}
are (purely) stably embedded in
M
such that
\mathcal{A}\perp\mathcal{C}
. For this, given
N\in\mathbb{N}
, we consider the structure
M_{N}
given by restricting
M
to the sorts
\mathcal{A}\sqcup\{\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{m}:m\leqslant N\}\sqcup\mathcal{C}
. For
m\leqslant N
we denote by
p_{N,m}
the structural map from
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{N}
to
\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{m}
and by
q_{N,m}
the one from
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{N}
to
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{m}
. For any
m\leqslant N
, the sequence
\tilde{S}_{m}
of
\tilde{R}
-modules

0\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{m}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{m}\rightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{C}}\rightarrow 0

is interpretable in the sequence
\tilde{S}_{N}
once a predicate for
\ker(q_{N,m})\leqslant\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{N}
is added. Thus,
M_{N}
may be seen as an
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{C}
-enrichment of
\tilde{S}_{N}

As in the previous proofs, it follows that
\mathcal{A}\cup\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta}
is stably embedded in
M
, with
(\mathcal{A}\cup\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{\Delta})\perp\tilde{\mathbf{C}}
.
Given
e\in M^{\mathrm{eq}}
, we choose
X\subseteq\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\times\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{k}^{n}

\mathcal{L}(M)
-definable with
e=\ulcorner X\urcorner
, where
\mathbf{A}^{\prime}
is a finite product of sorts from
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathbf{C}^{\prime}
is a finite product of sorts from
\mathcal{C}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
N\geqslant k
be such that

X
may be defined using formulas involving only variables from sorts in
\mathcal{A}\cup\mathcal{C}\cup\{\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{i}:i\leqslant N\}
. Since, for
N\geqslant m
,
\tilde{S}_{m}
is interpretable in an
\mathcal{A}
-
\mathcal{C}
-enrichment of
\tilde{S}_{N}
, we may conclude with
5.2.1
.
∎

5.3.
Imaginaries in RV

Recall that in a finitely ramified henselian valued field, the projective system of short exact sequences:

1\to\mathbf{R}_{n}^{\times}\to\mathbf{RV}_{n}^{\times}\to\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\times}\to 0

is stably embedded with the induced structure a
\mathbf{\Gamma}
-
\mathbf{R}
-enrichment of the pure short exact sequence of abelian groups. Thus
5.2.2
applies and yields the following elimination of imaginaries:

Proposition 5.3.1
.

Let
M
be a
\mathbf{\Gamma}
-
\mathbf{R}
-enriched finitely ramified henselian field,
A\subseteq\mathbfcal{G}(M)
,
e\in(\mathbf{RV}\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{A})^{\mathrm{eq}}(M)
and
E=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(e)
. Assume that:

•

For every
n,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
,
\mathbf{\Gamma}/\ell\mathbf{\Gamma}
is finite and the preimage in
\mathbf{RV}_{n}
of any coset of
\ell\mathbf{\Gamma}
contains an element which is algebraic over
\emptyset
.

Then
e\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{eq}}(E)\cup(\mathbf{Lin}_{A}\cup\mathbf{RV}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}(E)})^{\mathrm{eq}}(E))
.

In particular, for
A=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)\subseteq M^{\mathrm{eq}}
,
(\mathbf{RV}\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)})^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A))
.

Proof.

We apply
5.2.2
with
R=\tilde{R}=\mathbb{Z}
. Since
\mathbf{\Gamma}
is ordered is it a torsion free
\mathbb{Z}
-module, so (1) - (3) hold and (4) holds by hypothesis.
∎

These result also apply with an automorphism:

Proposition 5.3.2
.

Let
M\models\mathrm{VFA}^{\mathrm{mult}}_{0,0}
,
A\subseteq\mathbfcal{G}(M)
,
e\in(\mathbf{RV}\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{A})^{\mathrm{eq}}(M)
and
E=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(e)
. Then
