e\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A\mathbf{\Gamma}(E)\mathbf{RV}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}(E)}(E)\mathbf{Lin}_{A}(E))
.

In particular, for
A=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)\subseteq M^{\mathrm{eq}}
,
(\mathbf{RV}\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)})^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbfcal{G}(A))
.

Proof.

We apply
5.2.1
with
R=\mathbb{Z}[\sigma]
and
I:=\{P\in\mathbb{Z}[\sigma]:P(\mathbf{\Gamma})=0\}
, which is finitely generated since
R
is noetherian. Hypothesis (1) holds by assumption. Hypothesis (2) and (3) hold by multiplicativity: if
c\in\mathbf{\Gamma}_{>0}
and
P\in\mathbb{Z}[\sigma]
are such that
P(c)=0
, then for all
c\in\mathbf{\Gamma}
,
P(c)=0
and
P\in I
. Finally hypothesis (4) holds by divisibility.

So
e\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{eq}})(E)\cup(\mathbf{Lin}_{A}\cup\mathbf{RV}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}(E)})^{\mathrm{eq}}(E)
. But
\mathbf{\Gamma}
is an ordered vector field over (the field of fraction of)
\mathbb{Z}[\sigma]/I
, so it eliminates imaginaries. Also, by
Proposition

2.5.19
,
\mathbf{Lin}_{A}\cup\mathbf{RV}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}(E)}
weakly eliminate imaginaries. So
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{eq}}(E)\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf{\Gamma}(E))
and

(\mathbf{Lin}_{A}\cup\mathbf{RV}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}(E)})^{\mathrm{eq}}(E)\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf{Lin}_{A}(E)\mathbf{RV}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}(E)}(E)).

The result follows.
∎

6.
Imaginaries in valued fields

6.1.
The henselian case

Let
\mathrm{Hen}_{0}^{\mathrm{ac}}
denote the
\mathbf{RV}
-enrichment of
\mathrm{Hen}_{0}
with a compatible system of angular components
\mathrm{ac}_{n}:\mathbf{K}\to\mathbf{R}_{n}^{\times}
— we denote this language by
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{ac}}
. We fix
T
a
\mathbf{\Gamma}
-
\mathbf{k}
-enrichment of either
\mathrm{Hen}_{0}
or
\mathrm{Hen}_{0}^{\mathrm{ac}}
.

Recall that the two sorted language
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{mod}}
is given by a sort
\mathbf{A}
endowed with the language of rings, a sort
\mathbf{V}
endowed with the language of abelian groups and a function symbol
\mu:\mathbf{A}\times\mathbf{V}\rightarrow\mathbf{V}
for scalar multiplication. Let
t_{\ell}
denote the
\emptyset
-induced theory on
\mathbf{R}_{\ell}
. For every
X\subseteq\mathbf{V}^{2}
definable in the
\mathbf{A}
-enriched theory of free rank
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
modules over models of
t_{\ell}
, we define the equivalence relation
E_{X}
on
\mathbf{V}
by
vE_{X}w
if
X_{v}=X_{w}
and
\mathbf{T}_{n,\ell,X}:=\bigsqcup_{s\in\mathbf{S}_{n}}(\mathbf{V}/E_{X})^{(\mathbf{R}_{\ell},s/\ell\mathfrak{m}s)}
. Let
\mathbf{R}^{\mathrm{leq}}
denote
\bigsqcup_{n,\ell,X}\mathbf{T}_{n,\ell,X}
, the
\mathbf{R}
-linear imaginaries. We can now prove our imaginary Ax-Kochen-Ershov principle:

Theorem 6.1.1

(Theorem A)
.

Let
T
be a
\mathbf{\Gamma}
-
\mathbf{k}
-enrichment of either
\mathrm{Hen}_{0}
or
\mathrm{Hen}_{0}^{\mathrm{ac}}
, such that:

(
\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
)

T
has definably complete value group;

(
\mathbf{FR}
)

For every
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, the interval
[0,\mathrm{v}(\ell)]
is finite and
\mathbf{k}
is perfect;

(
\mathbf{I}_{\mathbf{k}}
)

The residue field
\mathbf{k}
is infinite;

(
\mathbf{E}^{\infty}_{\mathbf{k}})

The induced theory on
\mathbf{k}
eliminates
\exists^{\infty}
.

Then
T
weakly eliminates imaginaries in
\mathbf{K}\cup\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{eq}}\cup\mathbf{R}^{\mathrm{leq}}
.

Proof.

We will use
Proposition

4.4.8
. By
Theorem

3.1.3
, hypothesis
(
\mathbf{D}
)
holds. Hypothesis
(
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{K}}
)
holds trivially for
\mathcal{L}_{1}=\mathcal{L}
— and
f=\mathrm{id}
. Also,
\mathbf{RV}
and
\mathbf{R}
are stably embedded in characteristic zero henselian fields.
Let
M\models T
,
e\in M^{\mathrm{eq}}
and
A=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(e)
. By
Proposition

4.4.8
,
e\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf{K}(A)\cup(\mathbf{RV}\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)})^{\mathrm{eq}}(A))
.

Claim 6.1.2
.

(\mathbf{RV}\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)})^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A))

Proof.

If
T\supseteq\mathrm{Hen}_{0}^{\mathrm{ac}}
, then
\mathbf{RV}_{n}
is
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{ac}}
isomorphic to
\mathbf{R}_{n}^{\times}\times\mathbf{\Gamma}
and the isomorphisms are compatible as
n
varies. It follows that
(\mathbf{RV}\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)})^{\mathrm{eq}}\subseteq(\mathbf{\Gamma}\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)})^{\mathrm{eq}}
, and since
\mathbf{\Gamma}
and
\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)}
are orthogonal, the claim follows.

If
T
is a
\mathbf{\Gamma}
-
\mathbf{k}
-enrichment of
\mathrm{Hen}_{0}
, the claim follows from
Proposition

5.3.1
. Note that by
(
\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
)
,
\mathbf{\Gamma}\equiv\mathbb{Q}
or
\mathbf{\Gamma}\equiv\mathbb{Z}
and hence
\mathbf{\Gamma}/n\mathbf{\Gamma}
is finite and every coset is represented in
\mathbf{\Gamma}(\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\emptyset))
.
∎

Claim 6.1.3
.

\mathbf{Lin}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)}(A)\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf{R}^{\mathrm{leq}}(A))

Proof.

Recall that
\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)}
is stably embedded. It follows that, for every
e\in\mathbf{Lin}^{\mathrm{eq}}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)}(A)
, taking tensor products of lattices, we may assume that there exists
n,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
s\in\mathbf{S}_{n}(A)
such that
e
codes some subset
X_{a}
of
s/\ell\mathfrak{m}s
and
a
a single parameter in
s/\ell\mathfrak{m}s
. Since
s/\ell\mathfrak{m}s
is definably isomorphic to
\mathbf{R}_{\ell}^{n}
once we name a basis, it follows that
X
is definable with parameters in the
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{mod}}
-structure
(\mathbf{R}_{\ell},s/\ell\mathfrak{m}s)
— so
e\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf{T}_{n,\ell,X}(A))
.
∎

It follows that
e\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf{K}(A)\cup\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)\cup\mathbf{R}^{\mathrm{leq}}(A))
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Let
\mathbf{k}^{\mathrm{leq}}:=\bigsqcup_{s,n,X}(\mathbf{V}/E_{X})^{(\mathbf{k},s/\mathfrak{m}s)}
.

Corollary 6.1.4
.

Let
F
be a characteristic zero field that eliminates
\exists^{\infty}
. Then any valued field elementarily equivalent to
F((t))
or
F((t^{\mathbb{Q}}))
(with or without angular components) weakly eliminate imaginaries in
\mathbf{K}\cup\mathbf{k}^{\mathrm{leq}}
.∎

In certain cases, the elimination of imaginaries in
\mathrm{Th}(\mathbf{k})
-linear structures allows to further reduce this result to the geometric sorts. We can then also code finite sets, by adapting an argument of Johnson
[
Joh20
, Section 5.3]
:

Proposition 6.1.5
.

Let
M
be a henselian valued field such that:

(
\mathbf{I}_{\mathbf{k}}
)

The residue field
\mathbf{k}
is infinite;

(
\mathbf{Cb}_{\mathbf{K}}
)

For any
A=\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)\subseteq M^{\mathrm{eq}}
, any
\mathcal{L}(A)
-definable type
p\in\mathrm{S}^{0}_{\mathbf{K}^{n}}(M)
finitely satisfiable in
M
is
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\mathbfcal{G}(A))
-definable.

Then every finite set in
\mathbfcal{G}
is coded in
\mathbfcal{G}
.

Proof.

The proposition follows from:

Claim 6.1.6
.

Let
C\subseteq\mathbfcal{G}
be a finite set. There exists an
\mathcal{L}(\ulcorner C\urcorner)
-definable type
p_{C}\in\mathrm{S}_{\mathbf{K}^{n}}^{0}(M)
finitely satisfiable in
M
such that
C
is
\mathcal{L}(a)
-definable for any
a\models p_{C}
.

Indeed taking
p_{C}
as in the claim, by
(
\mathbf{Cb}_{\mathbf{K}}
)
,
p_{C}
is
\mathbfcal{G}(\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\ulcorner C\urcorner))
-definable and hence, so is
C
.

We now prove the claim. We start by considering
C=\{s\}\subseteq\mathbf{S}_{n}
.
By
(
\mathbf{I}_{\mathbf{k}}
)
, the generic type
q_{n}
of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathcal{O})
(that is, the quantifier
free type that reduces to the generic of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbf{k})
modulo
\mathfrak{m}
) is
finitely satisfiable in
M
. Note also that it is
\mathcal{L}_{0}
-definable and
symmetric (
cf.
,
[
Joh20
, Section 3.3]
): for every definable quantifier free type
r
,
q_{n}\otimes r=r\otimes q_{n}
. Let
B
be the matrix associated to some basis of
s
in

M
and let
p_{s}=B\cdot q_{n}
. This type does not depend on
B
, is

\mathcal{L}_{0}(s)
-definable, finitely satisfiable in
M
and symmetric.

If
C\subseteq\mathbf{S}_{n}
, let
a\models\bigotimes_{s\in C}p_{s}
and
A
be
the set of the
a_{s}
, for
s\in S
. Since finite subsets of
\mathbf{K}
are
coded in
\mathbf{K}
,
\ulcorner A\urcorner
can be identified with a tuple in
\mathbf{K}
. Then

p_{C}=\mathrm{tp}_{0}(\ulcorner A\urcorner/M)
does not depend on a choice of enumeration of
C

and thus it is
\mathcal{L}(\ulcorner C\urcorner)
-definable (and finitely satisfiable in
M
).
Note that if
\mathbf{\Gamma}(M)
has a smallest positive element, we are done since, for
any lattice
s
,
\mathfrak{m}s
is (
\mathcal{L}
-definably isomorphic to) a lattice and
hence
\mathbf{T}_{n}

\mathcal{L}
-definably embeds in
\mathbf{S}_{n+1}
by the usual
identification of translates of linear spaces with higher dimensional linear
spaces.

Let us now assume that
\mathbf{\Gamma}(M)
does not admit a smallest positive element. We
first consider the case where
C\subseteq\mathbf{K}^{i}\times\mathbf{k}^{j}
. Let
E\subseteq\mathbf{k}
be the set of elements of
\mathbf{k}
appearing as coordinates of
elements in
C
, let
b
be the tuple of coefficients of the polynomial
\prod_{e\in E}(x-e)
and let
p_{E}\in\mathrm{S}^{0}(M)
be the type of generic lifts of

b\in\mathbf{k}
to
\mathcal{O}
. This type is
\mathcal{L}(\ulcorner C\urcorner)
-definable and finitely satisfiable
in
M
since the value group does not admit a smallest positive element. Then
for any
a\models p_{E}
,
\mathrm{res}
induces a bijection between the
\mathcal{L}_{0}(a)
-definable set of roots of the polynomial
\sum_{\ell}a_{\ell}x^{\ell}
and
E
. It follows that
C
is in
\mathcal{L}_{0}(a)
-definable bijection with a subset
D
of

\mathbf{K}^{i+j}
, which is coded in some cartesian power of
\mathbf{K}
. Then
p_{C}=\mathrm{tp}_{0}(a\ulcorner D\urcorner/M)
has the required properties.

Let us finally consider
e\in\mathbf{T}_{n}(M)
and let
s=\tau_{n}(e)
(see page
2.2
). If
a\models p_{s}
, then
e
is
\mathcal{L}_{0}(a)
-definably isomorphic to a tuple
b\in\mathbf{k}
. The type
p_{e}=\mathrm{tp}_{0}(ab/M)
is
\mathcal{L}_{0}(e)
-definable, finitely satisfiable in
M
and symmetric — since
\mathbf{k}
is quantifier free stable. If
C\subseteq\mathbf{T}_{n}
, let
a\models\bigotimes_{e\in C}p_{e}
and
A\subseteq\mathbf{K}^{i}\times\mathbf{k}^{j}
be the set of the
a_{e}
, for
e\in C
. Then, applying the previous paragraph to
A
, we find
p_{C}
as required.
∎

The authors would like to thank Ehud Hrushovski for his insights on the correct statement of the following corollary.

Corollary 6.1.7
.

Let
F
be a characteristic zero field that is either algebraically closed, pseudofinite or real closed. Then any valued field
M
elementarily equivalent to
F((t))
or
F((t^{\mathbb{Q}}))
(with or without angular components), eliminates imaginaries in
\mathbfcal{G}
provided we add the following (imaginary) constants:

•

if
F
is real closed and the value group is a
\mathbb{Z}
-group with minimal positive element
\gamma_{0}
, a constant for a half line of
\mathbf{RV}_{1,\gamma_{0}}
;

•

if
F
is pseudofinite, constants for a generator of Galois of
F
— see
[
Hru12
, Section 5.9]
;

•

if
F
is pseudofinite and the value group is a
\mathbb{Z}
-group with minimal positive element
\gamma_{0}
, a constant for a
(\mathbf{k}^{\star})^{n}
-orbit in
\mathbf{RV}_{1,\gamma_{0}}
, for every
n\geqslant 1
.

Proof.

Let
A=\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)\subseteq M^{\mathrm{eq}}
. By
2.5.13
,
\mathbf{Lin}_{A}(M)
is a

\mathrm{Th}(\mathbf{k}(M))
-linear structure with flags. If
\mathbf{k}(M)
is algebraically
closed, by
[
Hru12
, Lemma 5.6]
, it eliminates imaginaries. If
\mathbf{k}(M)

is real closed,
\mathbf{Lin}_{A}
also eliminates imaginaries by
Propositions

2.5.21
,
2.5.24
and
2.5.25
. If
\mathbf{k}(M)
is pseudofinite and
\mathbf{\Gamma}(M)
is
divisible,
\mathbf{Lin}_{A}(M)
has roots and we conclude with

[
Hru12
, Theorem 5.10]
. If
\mathbf{\Gamma}(M)
is a
\mathbb{Z}
-group,
\mathbf{Lin}_{A}
does
not have roots, but by
[
Hru12
, Remark 5.8]
it suffices to insure the
existence of an
\emptyset
-definable
(\mathbf{k}^{\star})^{n}
-orbit inside each one
dimensional vector space of
\mathbf{Lin}_{A}
,
i.e.
, each
\mathbf{RV}_{1,\gamma}

with
\gamma\in\mathbf{\Gamma}(A)
. For every
n
, write
\gamma
as
i\gamma_{0}+n\delta
. By assumption, there exists
\emptyset
-definable

(\mathbf{k}^{\star})^{n}\cdot\xi\subseteq\mathbf{RV}_{1,\gamma_{0}}
. Then

(\mathbf{k}^{\star})^{n}\cdot\xi^{i}\zeta^{n}\subseteq\mathbf{RV}_{1,\gamma}
is independent of
the choice of
\zeta\in\mathbf{RV}_{1,\delta}
and thus
\emptyset
-definable.

It now follows from
Corollary

6.1.4
that
M
weakly eliminates imaginaries in
\mathbfcal{G}
and we conclude with
Proposition

6.1.5
.
∎

Not all of these results are new, although all of the statements with angular components are. The case of
\mathbb{C}((t^{\mathbb{Q}}))
just amounts to Haskel-Hrushovski-Macpherson’s result
[
HHM06
]
for
\mathrm{ACVF}
. The case of
\mathbb{R}((t^{\mathbb{Q}}))
is Mellor’s result
[
Mel06
]
for
\mathrm{RCVF}
and the case of
F((t))
, with
F
pseudofinite, is Hrushovski-Martin-Rideau’s result
[
HMR18
]
for pseudolocal fields — slightly improved since we only require algebraic constants in
\mathbf{RV}_{1}^{\mathrm{eq}}
and not in
\mathbf{K}
.

Corollary 6.1.8
.

Let
F
be a positive characteristic perfect field that eliminates
\exists^{\infty}
. Then
\mathrm{W}(F)
(with or without compatible angular components) weakly eliminate imaginaries in
\mathbf{K}\cup\mathbf{R}^{\mathrm{leq}}
.∎

It seems plausible that, if
F\models\mathrm{ACF}
, the
\mathbf{R}
-linear imaginaries can also be eliminated, yielding elimination of imaginaries in
\mathbfcal{G}
for
\mathrm{W}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\mathrm{a}})
. However, this remains an open problem.

6.2.
The
\sigma
-henselian case

Let us conclude with the description of the imaginaries in
\mathrm{VFA}^{\mathrm{mult}}_{0,0}
:

Theorem 6.2.1

(Theorem B)
.

The theory
\mathrm{VFA}^{\mathrm{mult}}_{0,0}
(with or without equivariant angular components) eliminates imaginaries in
\mathbfcal{G}
.

Proof.

Any model of
\mathrm{VFA}^{\mathrm{mult}}_{0,0}
is elementarily equivalent to a maximally complete one (
cf.

Remark

2.4.4
) and
hence
(
\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{B}}
)
holds. By
2.4.7
, the structure induced on
\mathbf{\Gamma}
is

o
-minimal. So
(
\mathbf{C}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}}
)
and
(
\mathbf{E}^{\infty}_{\mathbf{\Gamma}})
hold. Finally,
(
\mathbf{E}^{\infty}_{\mathbf{k}})
holds since

\mathrm{ACFA}
eliminates
\exists^{\infty}
. By
Theorem

3.1.1
,
(
\mathbf{D}
)
holds.
Hypothesis
(
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{K}}
)
follows from
2.4.3
, with
\mathcal{L}_{1}:=\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{RV}}\cup\{\sigma_{\mathbf{RV}}\}
and
f(x):=(\sigma^{n}(c))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}
. So,
by
Proposition

4.4.8
, for every
M\models\mathrm{VFA}^{\mathrm{mult}}_{0,0}
,
e\in M^{\mathrm{eq}}
and
A=\mathrm{acl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(e)
, we have
e\in\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbf{K}(A)\cup(\mathbf{RV}\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)})^{\mathrm{eq}}(A))
.
By
Proposition

5.3.2
— or using the angular components — we have

(\mathbf{RV}\cup\mathbf{Lin}_{\mathbfcal{G}(A)})^{\mathrm{eq}}(A)\subseteq\mathrm{dcl}^{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathbfcal{G}(A))
.
∎

Similar results hold in differential valued fields.

Corollary 6.2.2
.

The following two families of difference valued fields, indexed by integer primes
p
:

(1)

K_{p}:=(\mathbb{F}_{p}(t)^{\mathrm{a}},\mathrm{v}_{t},\phi_{p})
, where
\phi_{p}
is the Frobenius automorphism;

(2)

K_{p}:=(\mathbb{C}_{p},\mathrm{v}_{p},\sigma_{p})
, where
\sigma_{p}
is an isometric lift of the Frobenius automorphism on
\mathbf{k}(\mathbb{C}_{p})=\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\mathrm{a}}
.

uniformly eliminate imaginaries in
\mathbfcal{G}
for large
p
: for every
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{RV}}^{\sigma}
-definable sets
X\subseteq Y\times Z
, there exists an
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{RV}}^{\sigma}
-definable map
f:Z\to W
, where
W
is a product of sorts in
\mathbfcal{G}
and some
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}
such that, for every prime
p>N
and
z_{1},z_{2}\in Z(K_{p})
,
f(z_{1})=f(z_{2})
iff and only if
X_{z_{1}}(K_{p})=X_{z_{1}}(K_{p})
. ∎

As noted earlier, in case (1), since
K_{p}
is a definable expansion of a model of
\mathrm{ACVF}
which also eliminates imaginaries in the geometric sorts, the result is even uniform in all
p
.
